Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Question | Correct Answer | Explanation |
|----------|---------------|-------------|
| Q21 | A | Bash 5.2 hardened function import (post-shellshock), only BASH_FUNC_name%% env vars imported as functions, and changed
parser to avoid implicit eval. Plus added patsub_replacement shell option for & handling in ${var/pat/repl}. Extensively documented in CHANGES
2022. |
| Q22 | B | eval re-parses data as code -> command injection. If USER_INPUT contains ; rm -rf /;, it's executed. Fix: never eval untrusted input.
Use arrays: cmd=(echo "$USER_INPUT"), "${cmd[@]}", or printf '%q' to escape, or avoid eval entirely using ${!var} for indirect expansion. |
| Q23 | B | ${!map[@]} expands to keys of associative array. Must be quoted "${!map[@]}" to handle keys with spaces. ${map[@]} gives values,
not keys. declare -A requires Bash 4+. |
| Q24 | A | exec 3<> file opens fd 3 RW. flock -n 3 tries non-blocking exclusive lock on fd 3. If fails, another instance running -> exit. Classic Bash
mutex / singleton pattern, atomic and safe for cron jobs. |
| Q25 | B | Avoids fork/exec per line. Builtin [[ $line == *pattern* ]] is glob match in same process, ~50-100x faster than piping to grep each
iteration. read -r and IFS= preserve whitespace. |
| Q26 | B | mapfile/readarray builtin reads file lines into array in C code, not Bash loop. Orders of magnitude faster for large files. -t removes
delimiter (newline). Added Bash 4.0, essential for performance in 2024+. |
| Q27 | A | local var="value" scopes variable to function. Without local, all vars are global by default in Bash (unlike other languages). Must be first
statement in function. |
| Q28 | B | Transformation operators (Bash 4.4+): ${var@P} expands as prompt string (\u -> username etc), ${var@Q} outputs quoted so eval
could re-parse safely: e.g., var="a b" -> 'a b'. Others: @A (assignment), @a (attributes), @K (assoc array). |
| Q29 | B | mktemp creates unique file with 600 perms atomically (no race). $$ method is predictable and vulnerable to symlink attack. Always trap
EXIT to cleanup. Pattern: tmp=$(mktemp); trap 'rm -f "$tmp"' EXIT INT TERM. |
| Q30 | B | Normally ERR trap does NOT inherit into functions/subshells. set -E / errtrace makes ERR trap inherited, so echo "Failed at $LINENO:
$BASH_COMMAND" works inside functions too. Critical for debugging complex scripts. $LINENO and $BASH_COMMAND show where error
occurred. |

# PowerShell - Expert Certification Exam
**Course:** PowerShell 7.x Administration & Scripting (2024-2026 Syllabus)

D) Shows process list

A) ==

C) -eq
D) -ceq

C) $
D) &

A) Show-Help
B) Get-Help
C) Find-Help

--- PAGE 62 ---
Q8. Which pipeline operator sends output from one cmdlet to another?
A) |
B) >
C) >>
D) &

A) // comment
B) # comment
C) -- comment
D) /* comment */
Q10. What does $_ or $PSItem represent inside a pipeline?
A) The current user
B) The current object in the pipeline
C) The last error
D) The home directory
Answer Key - Beginner
1. B - PowerShell 7.4 LTS is built on .NET 8 and is cross-platform (Windows, Linux, macOS), open-source. Windows PowerShell 5.1 is
Windows-only on .NET Framework.
2. B - $PSVersionTable.PSVersion shows detailed version. Get-Host is less reliable.
3. B - Get-ChildItem lists items, aliases include ls/dir/gci.
4. C - -eq is case-insensitive equality by default. -ceq is case-sensitive. == is not PowerShell.
5. C - $ denotes variables. @ is for arrays/hashtables/splatting.
6. B - Get-Help, e.g., Get-Help Get-Process -Examples. Update-Help downloads help content.
7. C - RemoteSigned is the default on Windows Server: local scripts unrestricted, remote internet scripts need signature.
8. A - | is pipeline. > is redirection.
9. B - # for single-line. <# #> for block comments.
10. B - $_ / $PSItem is automatic variable for current pipeline object, e.g., Get-Process | Where-Object { $_.CPU -gt 100 }.

Q19. What does $ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop' do and how does it interact with

Q20. Which command lists only PowerShell modules available from PSGallery for

Answer Key - Intermediate
11. B - PowerShell comparison operators are case-insensitive by default. Prefix c = case-sensitive, i = explicit case-insensitive. So -eq == -ieq,
-ceq is case-sensitive.
12. B - ForEach-Object -Parallel was introduced in PS 7.0 (up to ThrottleLimit). foreach statement loads all into memory first, generally faster for
in-memory collections, but ForEach-Object streams pipeline and supports parallel.
13. B - Correct advanced function syntax uses [CmdletBinding()] and [Parameter(Mandatory)] with type and validation attributes.
14. B - PowerShell supports simplified syntax: Where-Object PropertyName -Operator Value. So Where-Object Length -gt 1MB works without {
$_.Length -gt 1MB }.
15. A - Get-CimInstance is modern replacement, uses WS-MAN, cross-platform. Get-WmiObject was removed from PowerShell 7+ (not available,
only in Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatibility).

--- PAGE 64 ---
16. B - PSNativeCommandArgumentPassing was experimental, now default in 7.4/7.5 improvements. It fixes infamous argument passing bugs
with native exe calls, using platform-specific quoting. Legacy mode can be forced with $PSNativeCommandArgumentPassing = 'Legacy'.
17. A - ?? is null-coalescing (returns left if not null else right), ??= is null-coalescing assignment, introduced in PS 7.0. Also ternary operator ? :
exists.
18. A - Get-Credential creates PSCredential. Best practice 2024-2026: SecretManagement + SecretStore vaults, or Azure Key Vault, avoid plain
text. Never hardcode.
19. B - By default many errors are non-terminating and don't trigger catch. Setting ErrorActionPreference to Stop or -ErrorAction Stop promotes
them to terminating exceptions catchable by try/catch.
20. A - Find-Module (PowerShellGet v2) and Find-PSResource (PSResourceGet, rebranded from PowerShellGet v3, included by default in 7.4+).
PSResourceGet is faster, more reliable.

Q27. How does PowerShell 7.5 on Linux/macOS handle $env:PATH vs Windows, and what

Q29. Given this PowerShell 7.x code: $data = @(); $data += Get-Process; $data |
Export-Csv file.csv -NoTypeInformation What is the performance problem and the modern
fix?
A) No problem
B) Array += rebuilds entire array each time (O(n^2)); use List<T> or $data = [System.Collections.Generic.List[object]]::new() and .Add(), or
ArrayList, or let pipeline output collection: $data = Get-Process (PowerShell already collects efficiently)
C) Export-Csv is deprecated
D) Must use For loop
Q30. You need to audit script execution, logging, and meet 2025 security baselines. Which
logging features should you enable for PowerShell?
A) No logging needed
B) Enable Module Logging, Script Block Logging (logs deobfuscated code to Event Log 4104), Transcription, AMSI integration, Constrained
Language Mode via WDAC/AppLocker, and use Purview/Auditing. PowerShell 7.4 logs to separate location vs Windows PowerShell Event Log
path: Microsoft-Windows-PowerShell/Operational and PowerShellCore/Operational
C) Only enable firewall
D) Delete logs regularly
Answer Key - Advanced
21. B - && and || are pipeline chain operators introduced in PowerShell 7.0. && executes RHS only if LHS succeeds ($? True), || only if LHS fails.
Exactly like bash. Very useful for succinct control flow.
22. B - ForEach-Object -Parallel -ThrottleLimit N is optimized for parallel runspaces in PS 7+. $using: scope to access outer variables. Start-Job is
heavier (separate process). Foreach -Parallel was workflow only (deprecated).
23. B - PowerShell classes use class keyword, constructor method named same as class. Supports inheritance, static members, hidden. Used for
DSC resources and type-safe models.
24. D - Strictly: ConvertFrom-Json implements RFC 8259 - no comments. PowerShell 7.4 didn't add JSONC support natively. Workaround: strip
with regex or use System.Text.Json with JsonCommentHandling.Skip, or use third-party. -AsHashtable returns hashtable instead of
PSCustomObject, -Depth increased default to 1024 in PS 7.4 (previously 2 caused truncation bugs).
25. B - Modern guidance 2024-2026: proper psd1 manifest with FunctionsToExport, CompatiblePSEditions = @('Desktop','Core'),
PowerShellVersion, PSScriptAnalyzer with settings (PSGallery), Pester v5 (BeforeAll/AfterAll, Should -Be), semantic versioning, and optionally
code signing.
26. B - Invoke-Expression is dangerous: if input concatenates user input, leads to injection similar to SQL injection. PSScriptAnalyzer rule
AvoidUsingInvokeExpression will warn. Use scriptblocks { } and & / Invoke-Command instead.
27. B - Never hardcode path separators. Join-Path works cross-platform. In PS 7.4+, Join-Path -Path a -AdditionalChildPath b,c,d allows multiple
children (previously only one). Use [IO.Path]::Combine or Join-Path. $env:PATH exists on all platforms but separator is : vs ; .
28. B - DSC Landscape 2024-2026: DSC v2 (WMF 5.1) with LCM still works but in maintenance. Microsoft released DSC v3 in 2024: new
architecture, cross-platform, standalone dsc.exe, no PowerShell dependency for execution engine, though resources can be PowerShell. Uses

--- PAGE 66 ---
DSC Resource manifest (dsc.resource.json). Recommended for new greenfield.
29. B - Classic PowerShell performance pitfall: arrays are fixed-size, += creates new array copying all elements = O(n²). For 10k processes,
severe. Use Generic List or pipeline assignment. Best: $data = @(); foreach ... { $data.Add($_) } or simply $data = Get-Process.
30. B - Security baseline: Turn on PowerShell Module Logging (captures pipeline execution), Script Block Logging (4104 - critical for detecting
obfuscated malware), Transcription (Start-Transcript logs I/O), AMSI (Antimalware Scan Interface - PS 5.1+ integrates with Defender),
Constrained Language Mode + WDAC. PS 7 logs to event log: PowerShellCore/Operational.

# VMware & Virtualisation - Expert Certification Exam

Section 1: Beginner (Questions 1-10)

B) DRS automatically vMotions AMD to Intel with no limits
C) DPM powers off vCenter

Q30. A VM snapshot chain is 250GB with 12 snapshots left for 60 days in production vSAN
ESA cluster. Impact and remediation in 2024 best practice?
A) Large snapshot chains cause performance degradation, high stun times on consolidation, increased RPO, space amplification; Best practice:
delete old snapshots via Snapshot Manager (consolidate), schedule maintenance window, use VMware Live Site Recovery or native backup with
CBT instead of long-lived snapshots, never use snapshots as backups
B) Snapshots improve performance
C) Keep snapshots indefinitely
D) Snapshots are automatically consolidated daily without issues
Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | A | RAID-5 FTT=1 needs 4 fault domains. 1 host loss = compliant but degraded. Rebuild requires remaining capacity; redundancy at risk until
replacement. |
| 22 | B | ESA RDMA introduced in vSAN 8 U2+ requires RoCE v2 NICs (100GbE preferred), NVMe, ESA cluster, enabled under vSAN services. |
| 23 | A | vLCM image-based is standard in 8.0: single desired image with ESXi version, add-ons, firmware/driver via hardware support manager. |
| 24 | A | Native Key Provider new in vSphere 7 U2+, enhanced in vSphere 8, removes need for external KMS for many use cases, supports TPM
2.0 attestation. |
| 25 | A | Advanced network troubleshooting: check driver, offload features (TSO, LRO, Geneve), physical NIC, NUMA, queue pairs. |
| 26 | A | vSphere 8 FT supports up to 8 vCPU (as of 8.0 U3), requires 10GbE+ logging network, continuous checkpointing. Multi-vCPU FT
revamped since vSphere 6.5. |
| 27 | A | OSA and ESA clusters cannot intermix in same cluster. Supported managed under same vCenter but as separate vSAN clusters. |
| 28 | A | vSphere Pod = secure VM sandbox (CRX - Container Runtime Executive). Full memory reservation enforces security/isolation, no
ballooning. |
| 29 | A | Cross-vendor vMotion (AMD<->Intel) not supported; must separate clusters. Cross-vCenter vMotion cold or powered-off migration only
across vendors. DPM works per cluster. |
| 30 | A | Long snapshot chains = classic VMware pitfall. Impacts: delta sprawl, consolidate stun can cause outages, vSAN space inefficient. Use
backups with CBT. |

B) You can write code directly without Main, file becomes entry point

B) x is null
C) x = null
D) x !=! null

B) Implicitly typed local variable, type inferred at compile time
C) Variant type that can hold any type like object
D) Alias for void

--- PAGE 73 ---
Q9. Which .NET garbage collector mode is default for console apps in .NET 8?
A) Server GC
B) Workstation GC with Concurrent GC
C) No GC
D) Manual GC only

A) static void Main()
B) static async Task Main()
C) static async void Main()

Answer Key - Beginner
1. B - int is an alias for System.Int32 in C#
2. B - record provides immutable reference types with value equality; C# 12 added primary constructors for record classes and structs
3. B - .NET 8 SDK templates target net8.0, enable ImplicitUsings, Nullable, and use top-level statements
4. B - With NRT enabled, string? denotes a nullable reference type
5. B - C# 12 introduced collection expressions [1,2,3] for arrays, spans, lists
6. B - Top-level statements allow code directly in Program.cs without boilerplate Main
7. B - is null is the idiomatic pattern matching null check, avoids operator overload issues
8. B - var is compile-time inferred, still strongly typed
9. B - Console/workstation apps use Workstation GC; ASP.NET Core uses Server GC by default
10. B - static async Task Main() or static async Task<int> Main() is supported since C# 7.1

Answer Key - Intermediate
11. B - C# 12 extended primary constructors to all classes and structs, parameters are in scope for whole class body
12. A - TimeProvider and FakeTimeProvider (.NET 8) enable deterministic testing of time-dependent code
13. A - required modifier enforces that members must be initialized via object initializer or constructor
14. B - stackalloc with Span<T> allocates on stack, low-overhead; in safe context allowed but cannot return/escape
15. B - IHostedLifecycleService extends IHostedService with 6 lifecycle methods for more granular startup/shutdown control
16. B - .NET 8 Blazor Web App unifies hosting models with per-component and per-page render modes, including enhanced SSR streaming
17. A - InlineArray (C# 12 / .NET 8) provides safe fixed-size buffers without unsafe fixed keyword
18. B - record struct = struct with synthesized equality; record or record class = reference type record
19. A - EF Core 8 introduced ComplexTypeAttribute for DDD value objects persisted inline, improved over Owned Entities
20. B - Native AOT compiles IL to native at publish, benefits startup and size; requires trim-compatible code, no dynamic Assembly.Load or
unannotated reflection

21. B - C# 13 expands params to allow ReadOnlySpan<T> and other collection types, improving performance by avoiding array allocation

27. B - OverloadResolutionPriorityAttribute (C# 13) lets APIs add new, more specific overloads and guide compiler to prefer them
28. B - Frozen collections optimize for read-heavy scenarios; after freezing, they use e.g. hash-table variants optimized for cache locality
29. B - .NET 9 added OrderedDictionary (generic), introduced new LINQ operators CountBy/ AggregateBy/ Index, and expanded
System.Linq.Async
30. A - await using var resource = ... for IAsyncDisposable; ConfigureAwait(false) still recommended for libraries to avoid deadlocks/context
marshaling, though ASP.NET Core no longer has SynchronizationContext

D) Delays compilation

go

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | Array: [3]int fixed size value type; Slice: []int dynamic descriptor (ptr, len, cap) referencing array |
| 12 | B | Slices share underlying array header; b and a point to same array, so mutation visible via a |
| 13 | B | defer pushes call onto stack executed LIFO when enclosing function returns |
| 14 | B | Go interfaces implemented implicitly (duck typing) - no implements keyword required |
| 15 | A | WaitGroup Add/Done/Wait synchronizes goroutines |
| 16 | A | slices.Clone (Go 1.21) makes shallow copy. context.WithCancel returns (child Context, CancelFunc) |
| 17 | B | Race = unsynchronized concurrent read+write. Use race detector flag -race |
| 18 | B | Close of closed channel => panic. Send on closed => panic. Receive from closed => zero value after buffer drained |
| 19 | B | go mod tidy ensures go.mod matches imports |
| 20 | B | Go 1.18+ generics use [T Constraint] syntax; monomorphization-like stenciling at compile time |

Q21. What is the difference between sync.Mutex and sync.RWMutex?

Q22. Explain memory model and goroutine leak scenario:
go
func leak() {

go func() {
val := <-ch
fmt.Println(val)
}()
// never send to ch
A) Leaked goroutine is blocked forever on channel receive; underlying stack not GC'd, use buffered channel/context or close
B) Goroutine will exit automatically after 1 second
C) Go runtime kills blocked goroutines after GC
D) No leak, channel GC'd

D) Loops now run in parallel

D) Stack size

C) Context is not safe for concurrent use

B) unsafe.Pointer is for deleting pointers; cgo is for compiling C++ only

Q27. How does Go's garbage collector work (as of Go 1.21-1.23)?
A) Reference counting only
B) Concurrent, tri-color mark-and-sweep, non-generational (generational preview via GOEXPERIMENT=greenteagc in 1.23), low pause
C) Stop-the-world only

Q30. What is the difference between buffered and unbuffered channels, and what is new
about range over func (Go 1.23)?

Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | RWMutex: RLock allows many readers concurrently; Lock exclusive. Use for read-heavy workloads |
| 22 | A | Unreceived channel = goroutine blocked forever => leak. Fix with context with timeout/cancel, buffered channel, or ensure send/close |
| 23 | B | Go 1.22 per-loop variable semantics changed (was GOEXPERIMENT=loopvar in 1.21). Fixes for _, v := range { go func(){ use v}() } bug |
| 24 | B | GOMAXPROCS controls P count (logical CPUs). Since Go 1.5 default NumCPU. Affects parallelism, not concurrency limit |
| 25 | A | Parent cancel -> children cancel via Done channel. Child cancel doesn't affect parent/sibs. Contexts are thread-safe, should be first
param, not stored |
| 26 | A | unsafe.Pointer for unsafe casts, e.g., *int to *float64, uintptr conversions. cgo: import "C" allows calling C |
| 27 | B | Go GC is concurrent mark-sweep, tri-color, with write barriers. Sub-ms STW. Go 1.23 experimental green tea GC is generational |
| 28 | B | Escape analysis detects x address escapes foo, allocates on heap, safe |
| 29 | B | Graceful shutdown pattern: signal.NotifyContext, then server.Shutdown with timeout lets in-flight requests finish |
| 30 | A | Unbuffered sync; buffered async up to cap. Go 1.23 range-over-func (iter package): for x := range mySeq where mySeq is iterator |

# Course Title: Java Programming - Comprehensive Assessment (2024-2026 Edition)

A) 16-bit

**Q10. What will happen if you try to compile: int x = 10; System.out.println(x/0); with integer division by zero?**

B) Compiles but throws ArithmeticException at runtime
C) Compilation error
D) Compiles and prints Infinity
Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Question | Answer | Explanation |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Q1 | B | public static void main(String[] args) is the standard entry point. Java 21 also allows simpler forms for unnamed classes preview, but B
remains canonical. |
| Q2 | C | final class cannot be extended. sealed restricts which classes can extend, but final prevents entirely. |
| Q3 | B | Instance booleans default to false, objects to null, numeric to 0. |
| Q4 | B | String is a class (object), not a primitive. 8 primitives: byte, short, int, long, float, double, char, boolean. |
| Q5 | B | javac is the Java compiler, java is the launcher. |
| Q6 | B | == compares references, not content. new String() creates new object, so reference comparison is false. |
| Q7 | B | Enhanced for-loop: for (String s : list) introduced in Java 5. |
| Q8 | B | Java int is always 32-bit regardless of platform (JVM spec). |
| Q9 | C | protected = package + subclasses (even in other packages). Default is package only. |
| Q10 | B | Integer division by zero compiles but throws java.lang.ArithmeticException: / by zero at runtime. Floating-point division would give
Infinity. |

Answer Key - Intermediate Level
| Question | Answer | Explanation |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Q11 | C | HashSet (Set interface) prohibits duplicates. List allows duplicates. |
| Q12 | B | Virtual Threads (JEP 444) became production in Java 21. Lightweight threads managed by JVM for high-throughput concurrency. |
| Q13 | B | ArrayList is not thread-safe. Concurrent modification leads to fail-fast behavior or data corruption. Use Collections.synchronizedList or
CopyOnWriteArrayList. |
| Q14 | B | substring(beginIndex inclusive, endIndex exclusive). Index 1='a', 2='v', so 1 to 3 = "av". |
| Q15 | B | Records (JEP 395, final in Java 16) provide immutable data carriers. Java 21 enhances with record patterns. |
| Q16 | B | try-with-resources requires AutoCloseable/Closeable. Compiler inserts close() call, handling suppressed exceptions. |
| Q17 | B | ArrayList backed by array -> O(1) get. LinkedList doubly-linked -> O(n) get but O(1) insert/delete if you have node reference. |
| Q18 | B | Sealed classes (JEP 409, Java 17) restrict permitted subclasses, enabling exhaustive pattern matching. Subclasses must be final,
sealed, or non-sealed. |
| Q19 | C | Both are valid lambda forms. A is explicit typed, B is inferred. D is invalid without braces. |
| Q20 | C | ofNullable(null) creates empty Optional. orElse returns fallback value, so "default". |

Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Question | Answer | Explanation |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Q21 | B | Virtual threads are cheap user-mode threads scheduled by JVM on carrier platform threads. Millions can be created. When they block
on I/O, carrier thread is freed. Revolution for high-concurrency servers. |
| Q22 | B | Type erasure: generics compile-time only. At runtime List<String> becomes List. Can't create new T() or instanceof List<String>. Need
TypeToken or Class<T>. |
| Q23 | B | Pattern matching for instanceof (JEP 394 final Java 16) plus pattern guards (Java 21). String s is binding variable available in scope. |
| Q24 | B | volatile guarantees happens-before visibility. But volatile int i; i++ is read-modify-write, not atomic. Need AtomicInteger or synchronized
for atomicity. |
| Q25 | B | Structured Concurrency (Preview Java 21+, JEP 480 in Java 23) simplifies concurrent programming: try (var scope = new
StructuredTaskScope.ShutdownOnFailure()) { ... }. Ensures lifetime management. |
| Q26 | B | Stream.toList() from Java 16 returns unmodifiable list (immutable). Unlike Collectors.toList() which returns mutable (spec does not
guarantee mutability but typically ArrayList). |
| Q27 | B | ZGC (JEP 333+) is low-latency GC. Generational ZGC (JEP 440/446) became default direction in Java 21+, maintaining <1ms pauses
even for multi-TB heaps. |
| Q28 | B | Record patterns (JEP 440 Final Java 21) allow deconstruction: instanceof Point(int x, int y). Also works in switch: case Point(int x, int y)
-> .... |
| Q29 | B | CompletableFuture = async composition (thenApply, thenCombine) but callback hell. Virtual Threads = Thread.startVirtualThread(() ->
blockingCall()) looks synchronous but scales to millions. Java 21 guidance: prefer virtual threads for I/O-bound concurrency, CF for reactive
pipelines. |

--- PAGE 86 ---
| Q30 | B | requires transitive gives implied readability: If module web requires transitive util, any module requiring web can use util without explicit
requires. Enhances API design. |

A) It's an alias for const

| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | Sync first: 1,5 then microtasks FIFO: queueMicrotask(2) then then(3), then macrotask setTimeout 4. |
| 22 | B | WeakRef doesn't prevent GC; FinalizationRegistry callback runs after GC (not guaranteed timing). |
| 23 | A | ES2024 adds .transfer() to move/resize ArrayBuffers with detachment for zero-copy. |
| 24 | A | Use null-prototype objects, Object.hasOwn(), and libraries or validation. Object.create(null) has no __proto__. |
| 25 | B | Import attributes: with { type: "json" } is new standard (ES2024/25). Old assert deprecated. |
| 26 | B | Explicit Resource Management proposal: using / await using auto calls [Symbol.dispose] on scope exit, like C# using / Python with. |
| 27 | B | Regular function this = obj =>10. Arrow this = lexical (window/global undefined in strict module) => undefined, typeof undefined is
"undefined". |
| 28 | B | New decorator spec (ES2022-2024) is different from legacy TS experimental. Context-based model. |
| 29 | A | ES2024 utility: const {promise, resolve, reject} = Promise.withResolvers() - cleaner than let res; new Promise(r=>res=r). |

**Answer Key - Beginner Level:**

2. B - def is used to define functions.
3. B - "Python 3.12" has 10 characters including space and dot.
4. C - list is mutable; tuple, str, frozenset are immutable.
5. B - // is floor division, 5 // 2 = 2.
6. C - # starts a single-line comment.
7. B - 0, empty string, and empty list are all falsy.
8. B - append() adds to the end.
9. A - input() is used in Python 3; raw_input was Python 2.
10. C - range(3) gives 0,1,2.

Q11. What is the output of: x = [1,2,3]; y = x; y.append(4); print(x)?

**Answer Key - Intermediate Level:**
11. B - Assignment does not copy list; both x and y refer to same object, so append affects x.
12. B - match-case introduced in Python 3.10 (PEP 634) is structural pattern matching.
13. C - *args collects extra positional args as tuple, **kwargs collects extra keyword args as dict.
14. A - Correct syntax: [expression for var in iterable if condition].
15. B - == value equality, is identity check.
16. C - except Exception catches Exception hierarchy, not BaseException subclasses like KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit.
17. B - @property makes method accessible as attribute with getter/setter.
18. B - fromkeys uses same object reference for all keys; mutating one affects all. Use dict comprehension for independent lists.
19. B - PEP 701 in Python 3.12 lifts many f-string restrictions (same quotes, multiline, reuse).
20. B - with statement uses context manager to guarantee __exit__ / file close.

B) Using pattern: (lambda: (yield 1)) defines generator function; calling it returns generator, next() yields 1

D) Returns lambda

A) Creates variable
B) PEP 695: New explicit type alias syntax

D) Error
Q27. What is MRO and which algorithm does Python use?
A) Method Resolution Order using Depth-First Search
B) Method Resolution Order using C3 linearization
C) Memory Recovery Order using BFS

Q28. What will import this do, and what about from __future__ import annotations in
Python 3.11-3.13?

B) import this causes error

Q29. What is the time complexity of looking up a key in a Python dict and set (CPython
3.12)?
A) O(n)
B) O(log n)
C) Average O(1), worst-case amortized O(n) due to hash collisions
D) O(n log n)
Q30. Explain: x = 256; y = 256; x is y vs x = 257; y = 257; x is y in CPython REPL?
A) Both True
B) Both False
C) First True due to small integer interning/cache [-5,256], second may be False (in script, may be True due to peephole optimization, but in
separate REPL lines typically False)
D) Syntax error
**Answer Key - Advanced Level:**
21. B - Python 3.13 introduced free-threaded experimental build (PEP 703) via python3.13t / --disable-gil; GIL remains default in standard build.

--- PAGE 101 ---
22. B - TaskGroup provides structured concurrency introduced in 3.11, with auto-cancel, ExceptionGroup (PEP 654) vs gather which aggregates
results.
23. B - Mutable default argument pitfall: default list is created once at function definition time.
24. B - __slots__ restricts __dict__ creation, saves memory, faster attribute access.
25. B - lambda: (yield 1) defines a generator function; calling it returns generator object; next() yields 1. Valid in Python 3.8+. (Note: yield inside
lambda clarified in modern Python).
26. B - PEP 695 in Python 3.12 introduces type statement for type aliases and generic type parameters def func[T](x:T).
27. B - MRO uses C3 linearization algorithm to resolve method lookup in multiple inheritance.
28. A - import this prints Zen; from __future__ import annotations (PEP 563) stores annotations as strings, postponed evaluation.
29. C - dict/set lookup is average O(1), worst O(n) on hash collisions (mitigated by randomization).
30. C - CPython caches small integers -5 to 256; 256 is cached so is True, 257 outside cache typically False in REPL interactive distinct objects.

B) Manages form action state: takes an action function and initial state, returns current state and callable action, integrates with useFormStatus for
pending/error handling
C) Only works with Redux
D) Creates animation state
Q16. What is the purpose of forwardRef and what is new in React 19 about refs?
A) Refs no longer exist
B) forwardRef allowed passing refs through components; In React 19, ref is a regular prop, forwardRef is not needed - you can just do function
MyComp({ref}) or receive ref as prop directly
C) forwardRef is required for all components now
D) Refs can only hold strings in React 19
Q17. How does useDeferredValue differ from useTransition?
A) Same thing
B) useDeferredValue defers the *value* (useful for debouncing search input rendering), while useTransition defers the *state update*. Both mark
updates as non-urgent
C) useDeferredValue is synchronous
D) useDeferredValue is deprecated
Q18. You have a context performance issue: every context value change re-renders all
consumers even if they only use part of value. Best 2024-2026 solution?
A) Ignore it
B) Split contexts, memoize value with useMemo, or use external store like Zustand/Jotai, or use context selector pattern (proposed) /
useContextSelector from third-party
C) Use Redux only
D) Use multiple useEffect
Q19. What does this React 19 code do? const data = use(fetchPromise)?
A) Invalid syntax
B) New use() API: can read Promise or Context conditionally unlike other hooks, suspends until promise resolves (integrates with Suspense)
C) Fetches data synchronously
D) Only works inside useEffect
Q20. Why should you NOT define components inside other components?
A) It's a syntax error
B) On every parent render, the nested component type is new, causing React to unmount/remount the child tree, losing state and hurting
performance
C) It makes code run faster
D) ESLint forbids it in React 19 compiler mode only
Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | useLayoutEffect blocks paint; use for measurements. useEffect is non-blocking. |
| 12 | B | useTransition enables concurrent rendering for non-urgent updates. |
| 13 | B | React Compiler (beta 2024, stable 2025) auto-applies memoization via Babel plugin. Manual memo still works but often redundant. |
| 14 | B | RSC = server-only, async, zero client JS by default. |
| 15 | B | useActionState (renamed from useFormState) is React 19 core for actions. |
| 16 | B | React 19 simplifies ref handling: ref is prop. forwardRef remains for compat but deprecated pattern. |
| 17 | B | DeferredValue = value-based defer, Transition = update-based control. |
| 18 | B | Context splits & memoization are standard fix; selectors pattern emerging. |
| 19 | B | use() is React 19's new hook that can be called conditionally and unwraps promises/contexts. |
| 20 | B | Component identity matters - nested definitions break reconciliation. |

Q21. What is React Compiler (formerly React Forget) and how does it change optimization
in 2025?
A) A new bundler replacing Webpack
B) A build-time optimizing compiler (Babel plugin/estree) that auto-memoizes components and hooks, eliminating need for manual useMemo,

C) A runtime JIT compiler
D) A TypeScript fork

Q26. What is the correct concurrent feature pattern for data fetching in 2024-2026 React?

Q29. In React 19.1 (May 2025) and Next.js 15, what is the behavior of "use cache"

Q30. Complex reconciliation question: You render <>{items.map(i=><MyComponent
key={i.id} id={i.id}/>)}</> where MyComponent is memoized with React.memo. If parent
re-renders but items array reference is same (e.g., from cache), and prop id unchanged,
will MyComponent re-render in React 19 without compiler and with compiler?
A) Without compiler: Yes, always re-renders; With compiler: No
B) Without compiler: No, memo prevents re-render since props shallow-equal; With compiler: Also No, but compiler makes memo automatic even
if you didn't wrap - both avoid re-render. If items array reference same but internal object mutated, still no re-render unless prop changes.

Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | Compiler auto-memoizes. Enabled via eslint-plugin-react-compiler + Next config. |
| 22 | A | Server Components cannot use state/effects/browser APIs. Must add "use client". |
| 23 | B | Date is classic hydration mismatch - must be client-only or suppressed. |
| 24 | B | useOptimistic is flagship React 19 hook for optimistic updates with Actions. |
| 25 | B | React 19 clean-up: defaultProps for functions deprecated long ago, propTypes extracted. Use default params + TS. |
| 26 | B | Modern pattern is render-as-you-fetch with Suspense, not fetch-on-render useEffect waterfall. |
| 27 | B | React 19 ref cleanup is important new feature for observers/listeners attached to DOM node. |
| 28 | A | Object identity breaks memo. UseMemo or Compiler or split contexts. |
| 29 | B | "use cache" is emerging RSC caching primitive (Next.js 15 canary docs, React 19.1). |
| 30 | B | memo does shallow prop check. Same id -> no re-render. Compiler automates memo. Need stable reference & no mutation. |

# Course Title: Git & GitHub - Complete Mastery Exam (2024-2026 Edition)
---
Section 1: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
Assesses fundamentals of Git, basic workflow, and GitHub basics.
**Q1. What command initializes a new Git repository in your current directory?**
A) git start
B) git init
C) git new
D) git create
**Q2. Which command stages a file named app.js for the next commit?**
A) git commit app.js
B) git stage app.js
C) git add app.js
D) Both B and C are correct
**Q3. What is the purpose of git clone <url>?**
A) Creates a new branch from the remote
B) Downloads a copy of an existing remote repository to local
C) Syncs local changes to GitHub
D) Deletes the remote repository
**Q4. In GitHub, what is a Pull Request (PR)?**
A) A request to pull the latest changes from remote to local
B) A proposal to merge changes from one branch into another, enabling review
C) An automatic backup of your repository
D) A command to delete a branch on GitHub
**Q5. What does git status show you?**
A) Your GitHub notifications
B) The commit history graph
C) The state of working directory and staging area, including untracked/modified files
D) List of all branches
**Q6. What is the .gitignore file used for?**
A) To ignore merge conflicts
B) To tell Git which files/folders to not track
C) To hide commits from GitHub
D) To ignore remote repositories
**Q7. What is the difference between git fetch and git pull?**
A) Both are identical
B) fetch downloads remote changes without merging, pull = fetch + merge into current branch
C) fetch uploads changes, pull downloads
D) fetch only works with GitHub CLI
**Q8. How do you create and switch to a new branch named feature-login in one command (Git 2.23+ preferred way)?**
A) git branch feature-login
B) git checkout -b feature-login
C) git switch -c feature-login
D) Both B and C work, C is the modern preferred syntax
**Q9. What does git commit -m "Add login page" do?**
A) Saves staged changes locally with a descriptive message
B) Immediately pushes changes to GitHub
C) Creates a new branch called "Add login page"
D) Stages all files and commits
**Q10. On GitHub, what does forking a repository do?**
A) Creates your own personal copy of someone else's repository under your account
B) Deletes the original repository
C) Creates a branch in the original repository
D) Downloads the repo as a ZIP file

--- PAGE 124 ---
Answer Key - Beginner
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 1 | B | git init creates a hidden .git directory and initializes a repo. |
| 2 | D | git add is the classic command; git stage is a synonym since Git 2.16+. |
| 3 | B | Clone copies the entire remote repo including history. |
| 4 | B | PR is core GitHub collaboration workflow for code review before merging. |
| 5 | C | git status is your primary tool for seeing staged/unstaged/untracked changes. |
| 6 | B | .gitignore prevents node_modules, .env, build artifacts etc. from being tracked. |
| 7 | B | Best practice: fetch first to review, then merge/pull. |
| 8 | D | checkout -b is legacy but still works; switch -c is introduced in Git 2.23 as clearer. |
| 9 | A | Commit is local only; push is required to send to remote. |
| 10 | A | Fork enables open-source contribution workflow without write access to original. |

**Q20. How do you undo the last commit but keep its changes staged, to edit the commit message or add more files?**

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | Merge brings feature into current checked-out branch. |
| 12 | A | Merge preserves context; rebase is cleaner but rewrites history - never rebase public/shared branches. |
| 13 | D | restore --staged is new since 2.23, reset is older but still common. |
| 14 | B | Stash creates a stack; use git stash pop or apply to restore. |
| 15 | B | Conflicts require manual resolution. Tools like VS Code help, but markers must be resolved. |
| 16 | B | Cherry-pick is useful for hotfixes - picking one commit without merging whole branch. |
| 17 | A | Introduced to enforce team quality gates: required reviews, CI passing, no force push on main. |
| 18 | B | Essential for understanding branching structure; --all shows all branches. |
| 19 | A | GitHub Actions workflows live in that directory and run on push/PR/schedule. |
| 20 | C | --soft moves HEAD but keeps index and working dir. --hard deletes changes, --mixed (default) unstages. |

A) Both are identical

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | Since 3.8+ Quorum Queues (Raft) are recommended; Classic Mirrored Queues removed in 4.0. Quorum provides data safety and
modern replication |
| 12 | C | Topic exchange uses * (one word) and # (zero or more words) wildcards |
| 13 | B | Publisher confirms ensure broker received responsibility for message, essential for no data loss |
| 14 | B | Streams = log abstraction, non-destructive consuming, offsets, high throughput - competing with Kafka use cases |
| 15 | B | DLX routes dead messages via x-dead-letter-exchange args |
| 16 | A | RabbitMQ 4.0 introduced Khepri (Raft-based metadata store) as default alternative to Mnesia for better clustering |
| 17 | B | basic.qos prefetch defines how many unacked messages a consumer can have |
| 18 | B | Unacked messages are requeued on channel/connection close |
| 19 | D | Best answer D: Shovel (reliable point-to-point transfer) and Federation (exchange-level federation) are DR tools; both valid. As of
2024-2026, recommended for WAN |
| 20 | B | Combination of durability + persistence + confirms + manual ack + replicated quorum queues = at-least-once |

Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | Quorum queues use Raft: needs majority (quorum) to be available; tolerates floor((n-1)/2) failures |
| 22 | B | Super Streams = partitioned append log; producer uses partitioning strategy, consumer super-stream aware for scalability while
preserving per-key order |
| 23 | B | SAC (x-single-active-consumer) ensures single active consumer with standby consumers for HA ordered consumption |
| 24 | B | Correct retry pattern avoids breaking FIFO order; immediate requeue is anti-pattern; use dead-lettered delay or per-key retry topic |
| 25 | B | Credit flow + memory/disk alarms block publishers; clients must handle Connection.Blocked and backpressure |
| 26 | B | x-delivery-limit (quorum queue only) solves poison message problem - core feature since 3.12 |
| 27 | B | OAuth2.0 / OIDC is now first-class in 3.11-4.x with scope to permission mapping |
| 28 | A | MQTT topic mapping to amq.topic with QoS translation and retained message store is standard interop |
| 29 | B | Streams/Super Streams are designed for high-throughput replay/fan-out, outperforming quorum queues for this workload |
| 30 | B | Proper rolling upgrade procedure in 4.x requires drain, quorum health checks, and orderly transfer of leadership |

8.13+)
C) Only via external plugin
D) Only exact brute-force search
Q25. What is RRF (Reciprocal Rank Fusion) introduced in Elasticsearch 8.13+?
A) A Logstash filter for log ranking
B) A method to combine results from multiple retrievals (e.g., BM25 text search + kNN vector search) for hybrid search; enables semantic + lexical
ranking via rank: { rrf: {} } in _search API
C) A Kibana role
D) A type of snapshot repository
Q26. You see CircuitBreakingException: [parent] Data too large and heap usage 95%.
Which actions are correct for production triage in 2024-2026?
A) Increase heap to 32GB+ and ignore
B) Check: 1) Aggressive aggregations on high-cardinality fields - use composite, limit size, 2) Large requests / bulk - reduce batch size, 3)
Fielddata on text fields - should be keyword, 4) Check for deep pagination from: 100000, use search_after / PIT, 5) Unrouted queries hitting too

--- PAGE 136 ---
many shards - reduce fan-out with routing or data streams filtering, 6) Review heap dump / nodes stats, enable real_memory circuit breaker,
consider off-heap (allocators). Never cross 50% of RAM for heap, keep <32GB to avoid compressed oops issue
C) Delete all indices
D) Restart Kibana
Q27. What is the ES|QL LOGSDB index mode introduced in 8.16+ and its benefit?
A) A new Kibana theme
B) index.mode: logsdb - optimized storage for log data using synthetic source, index.mapping.source.mode: synthetic, index.sort on @timestamp
+ host, reduced doc_values overhead, and default host.name constant_keyword optimizations; results in 30-50% storage reduction and up to 2-3x
faster queries for logs compared to standard index
C) A Logstash input plugin
D) A deprecated feature
Q28. Explain Elasticsearch Serverless (announced 2024, GA 2025) architecture vs classic
self-managed/Cloud hosted.
A) Same as classic
B) Serverless decouples compute and storage, auto-scales, stateless indexing/search tiers, object storage (S3) as primary durability layer with
local caching, eliminates need to size shards/nodes manually, offers Data Streams with data lifecycle management, billed by
search/indexing/storage consumed, while classic/Cloud hosted requires manual node sizing, shard planning, ILM configuration
C) Serverless does not support Kibana
D) Serverless only supports text search
Q29. How do you secure Logstash-to-Elasticsearch communication in 8.x with
security-on-by-default?
A) HTTP plain with no authentication
B) Configure TLS (CA cert from Elasticsearch), use API Keys or service tokens (preferred over basic auth for Logstash since 8.x), set
xpack.monitoring.enabled with appropriate credentials, and use cloud.id / cloud.auth if on Elastic Cloud; avoid embedding superuser elastic user
C) Disable security
D) Use only beats input with no SSL
Q30. A critical incident: ingestion lag is 2 hours, Logstash persistent queue is full (disk
95%), Elasticsearch rejects with 429 - EsRejectedExecutionException - queue full. What is
the correct remediation sequence aligned with Elastic 2024-2026 guidance?
A) Increase Logstash queue size indefinitely
B) 1) Scale Elasticsearch data nodes / hot tier, check thread_pool write queue, add nodes or reduce shard contention, 2) Add backpressure
handling - enable Logstash persistent queues + dead letter queues, reduce bulk sizing pipeline.batch.size, add Kafka/Redis as buffering layer, 3)
Enable index pressure memory limits and check for mapping explosion/nested loops, 4) Temporarily scale output workers, enable flow control in
Beats, 5) Long-term: use Data Stream lifecycle to rollover and isolate, use ILM searchable snapshots to free hot tier. Never drop rejected without
DLQ
C) Delete Logstash
D) Switch to debug logging only
Answer Key - Advanced
21. **B** - Modern architecture uses Data Streams + logsdb + synthetic source + tiered searchable snapshots.
22. **B** - Synthetic _source reconstructs from doc_values, saves storage.
23. **B** - filter context = no scoring, cacheable, faster.
24. **B** - dense_vector with HNSW, int8/bbq quantization, knn API, RRF for hybrid search, ELSER/E5 integration.
25. **B** - RRF fuses BM25 + kNN rankings for hybrid search.
26. **B** - Triage aggregations, deep pagination, fielddata misuse, bulk size, shard fan-out; heap best practices <32GB.
27. **B** - logsdb mode optimized for logs: synthetic source + sorted indices + storage saving.
28. **B** - Serverless: disaggregated, auto-scaling, object-store native, consumption-based billing vs manual sizing in classic.
29. **B** - Use TLS + API Keys/service tokens, avoid elastic superuser.
30. **B** - Scale ES write tier, backpressure via queue/DLQ/Kafka, fix mapping explosion, manage persistent queue and bulk sizing.

| Q2 | C | Actually global default is 1m? No, Prometheus default is 1m globally but 15s is common in docker/prometheus.yml. Updated: As of 2024
docs, default scrape_interval is 1m if not set, but many distributions set 15s. Correct standardized answer: D) 1 minute is the documentation

| Q6 | B | Counter is monotonically increasing. |
| Q7 | B | up is auto-generated for each target scrape health. |
| Q8 | B | Prometheus evaluates rules; Alertmanager handles deduplication, grouping, routing. |
| Q9 | B | Exporters translate existing metrics to Prometheus exposition format. |
| Q10 | B | Panels are visualization blocks within dashboards. |
*Corrected Q2 Note for 2024-2026: Prometheus server documentation states scrape_interval: 1m as true default if omitted. Many helm charts
override to 15s/30s. This exam uses 15s as practical expected answer aligned with Prometheus official prometheus.yml example file.*

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q11 | B | rate() calculates per-second avg increase rate over time window, handling counter resets. irate is instant rate from last two points. |

| Q13 | A | SD configs query APIs (K8s, EC2, Consul) to dynamically build scrape targets. |
| Q14 | B | Library panels enable reusability and single-source-of-truth updates across dashboards. |
| Q15 | A | Correlations creates clickable links across observability signals (metrics→logs→traces). |
| Q16 | B | Recording rules improve query performance and create new aggregated metrics. |
| Q17 | B | Prometheus 3.0 added UTF-8 support (with ?utf8-names flag), OpenTelemetry compatibility, revamped UI, and significant query
performance gains. |
| Q18 | B | These functions manipulate labels for presentation/joining, e.g., extracting parts of a label. |

--- PAGE 141 ---
| Q19 | A | Unified Alerting supports Grafana-managed (central) and Data-source-managed (Mimir, Loki, Prometheus) alerts. |
| Q20 | B | avg_over_time(up[5m]) < 0.9 implies <90% up over 5m, i.e., >10% down. While C is also logically similar using instant vector, B is more
robust with time window for alerting rule. Accepted correct: B (and C is variant). Official exam answer: B |

Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q21 | B | Use Thanos Ruler or Mimir Ruler with ring/election to avoid duplicate eval. Alertmanager dedup is second line but not primary solution
for HA rules. |
| Q22 | B | Exemplars allow trace exemplars - trace ID is stored as exemplar attached to histogram bucket/sample, enabling metric -> trace jump in
Grafana Tempo. |
| Q23 | B | High cardinality is #1 cause of Prometheus failure. Best practice: drop/relimit at ingestion, sample_limit, label_limit, limit per metric. |
| Q24 | B | Semantic difference: by is include-list, without is exclude-list, critical for correct aggregations. |
| Q25 | A | Scenes is Grafana's new dashboard framework from Grafana 10+ powering dashboard scenes, dynamic panels, and better
performance. |
| Q26 | B | Mimir microservices + object storage is 2024-2026 standard for massive multi-tenant long-term Prometheus; Thanos is also valid but
Mimir is purpose-built for active series. |
| Q27 | B | Native Histograms drastically reduce buckets and cardinality vs classic le buckets, with new schema. |
| Q28 | B | Always aggregate numerator and denominator separately with sum by after rate, filtering status via regex, avoiding many-to-many and
NoData issues. |
| Q29 | B | Multi-window multi-burn-rate is industry standard (SRE). Implementation via recording rules + 2 windows per burn rate. Grafana SLO
plugins use this. |
| Q30 | B | In Prometheus 3.x, head series not found + OOO samples relate to out-of-order ingestion, target limits, and scrape class changes -
check TSDB head, WAL, and OOO time window flag. |

# SRE & Observability - Certification Exam

D) Resource Throughput Objective

A) Microsoft Paint

A) A book for running marathons

Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 1 | B | Site Reliability Engineering - discipline founded at Google in 2003 by Ben Treynor Sloss |
| 2 | C | Four Golden Signals are Latency, Traffic, Errors, Saturation. Throughput is related to Traffic but not named as such. |
| 3 | B | SLO is internal target, tighter than SLA which is customer-facing contract |
| 4 | A | MTTR = Mean Time To Recovery/Repair/Resolve - key incident metric |
| 5 | C | Error budget = 100% - SLO; when exhausted, feature freezes until reliability improves |
| 6 | C | Counter is monotonically increasing; Gauge can go up/down |
| 7 | A | RTO is time target, RPO is data loss target |
| 8 | B | ELK/OpenSearch and Grafana Loki dominate log aggregation as of 2024-2026 |
| 9 | B | Toil is manual, repetitive, automatable, tactical, no enduring value, scales linearly |
| 10 | B | Runbooks enable faster, consistent incident response |

| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | A | 99.9% = 0.1% error budget = 43m49s in 30d (43200 min *0.001). Calculation is uptime/total time |

| 16 | B | Trace = DAG of spans representing full request; Span = timed operation with parent-child relationship |

positives |
| 18 | B | Chaos Engineering principles: build confidence, inject turbulence in controlled way; tools LitmusChaos, Chaos Mesh CNCF graduated in

--- PAGE 146 ---
| 20 | B | Standard SLI formula: SLI = valid events that were good / total valid events. Used for availability, latency SLIs |

A) eBPF requires more sidecars

C) eBPF only works on Windows
D) eBPF cannot do tracing
Q25. Your service uses Kubernetes. You need to implement SLO-compliant autoscaling
that considers custom SLI metric (queue depth + p95 latency) without flapping. What is
the 2024-2026 best practice?
A) Use only HPA on CPU
B) Use KEDA + HPA with custom metrics from Prometheus Adapter, with stabilization windows, scale-down delays, and SLO-aware thresholds
derived from error budget
C) Manual scaling
D) Disable autoscaling
Q26. Designing a postmortem / incident review: Which approach aligns with blameless

A) Find who caused outage and punish
B) Blameless postmortem focusing on systemic factors, contributing causes, timeline, mitigations, and action items using frameworks like
Learning Review; share widely
C) Don't write postmortems
D) Write postmortems only for >24h outages
Q27. In the context of Service Mesh (Istio, Linkerd) and SRE, how do you achieve SLI

A) Cannot isolate
B) Use fault injection, traffic shadowing, rate limiting, and per-route/tenant metrics with Envoy filters + OTel to compute per-tenant SLIs/SLOs, plus
priority/criticality levels

--- PAGE 147 ---
C) Give each tenant separate data center
D) Block all multi-tenant traffic
Q28. What is the significance of WCAG + Continuous Verification (e.g., Keptn, Argo

A) Unrelated to SRE
B) Automated canary analysis using SLI-based verification (metrics, traces) to auto-promote/rollback releases based on SLOs - e.g., Argo

C) Only for accessibility
D) Manual verification only
Q29. You have to choose between Thanos vs Cortex vs Mimir for long-term Prometheus
storage in 2025. Mimir is often preferred. Why?
A) Mimir is not scalable
B) Mimir offers microservices architecture, high cardinality handling better than old Cortex, native PromQL, high availability, and Grafana Labs
maintains it as successor to Cortex with 1B+ active series scale
C) Thanos is always better
D) Cortex is deprecated fully with no replacement
Q30. An organization wants to move from monitoring to observability maturity (Charity
Majors' model). What differentiates high observability?
A) More dashboards
B) Ability to ask arbitrary questions about system without shipping new code; emphasis on high-cardinality, wide structured events, trace-driven
debugging, and debugability over known-knowns dashboards
C) More alerts
D) Only metrics matter
Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | Multi-window multi-burn-rate: Google SRE Workbook recommends alert when fast burn (1h) AND slow burn (6h) both firing. e.g., page at
14x/6h+1h consumes 2% budget in 1h. Prevents flapping. |
| 22 | B | Connectors (stable 2024) bridge pipelines: e.g., count connector produces metrics from spans, enabling span-to-metrics |
| 23 | B | Hedged requests sends second request after p95 latency, cancels slow one. Adaptive concurrency (Vegas/BBR), jittered backoff avoids
retry storms. Circuit breaker prevents cascade. |
| 24 | B | eBPF runs in kernel, <1-2% overhead, no app change, gives L3-L7 telemetry, zero sidecar tax. Pixie auto traces, Cilium Hubble flow logs.
|
| 25 | B | KEDA scales on event source (queue), HPA v2 with custom.metrics.k8s.io + stabilizationWindowSeconds 300s, SLO-aware thresholds
prevent scaling beyond error budget. 2024-26 pattern. |
| 26 | B | Blameless postmortems per SRE book + Etsy Debriefing Facilitation guide. Focus on systems, psychological safety, learning review.
Google DORA metrics show this improves MTTR. |
| 27 | B | Per-tenant SLIs via Istio telemetry with custom dimensions (x-tenant-id header), rate limit filter, priority queue. Enables noisy-neighbor
detection and SLO isolation. |
| 28 | B | Progressive delivery + continuous verification = automated SLO check in deployment. Argo Rollouts AnalysisTemplate queries
Prometheus/ Kayenta checks. Prevents bad release promotion. |
| 29 | B | Mimir (AGPL) is Grafana's horizontally scalable TSDB, 1B series, separates read/write, better compaction vs Thanos gateway.
Recommended in 2024 CNCF survey for large scale. Thanos still good for existing Prometheus with object store. |
| 30 | B | Observability maturity: from known unknowns dashboards to unknown unknowns exploration. Requires structured logs, wide events
(Honeycomb canonical log), HCL, trace-first. Charity Majors O11y 2.0. |

# Splunk Core Certified User / Power User / Enterprise Administration - Certification Exam
> 30 Questions - Aligned to Splunk Platform 9.x - 9.2 / 9.3 and Splunk Cloud Platform (2024-2026), SPL2, Data Manager

Section 1: Beginner (Questions 1-10)

B) where

A) stats
B) top
C) rare
D) Both A and B can, but top is purpose-built for frequency

A) To define user permissions
B) To tell Splunk how to parse and interpret incoming data format (line breaking, timestamp, field extractions)

D) To encrypt data in transit

A) Search Head
B) Forwarder
C) Indexer
D) Deployment Server

D) search sourcetype=linux_secure failed password

--- PAGE 149 ---
Q9. What are Buckets in Splunk indexing?
A) User roles that control access
B) Directories containing indexed data, transitioning from hot -> warm -> cold -> frozen
C) Configuration containers for apps
D) Search result caches
Q10. What is the purpose of Splunk Apps and Add-ons?

D) To delete old data

Answer Key - Beginner
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 1 | B | Forwarders collect data (log files, metrics) at source and send to indexers. Heavy/Universal Forwarder distinction is key in 9.x. |
| 2 | C | search does keyword/metadata filtering early; where evaluates eval expressions (e.g., where bytes>1000). Both filter but differently. |
| 3 | C | inputs.conf enables monitoring: [monitor:///var/log/...]. outputs.conf controls forwarding destination. |
| 4 | B | _time, host, source, sourcetype, index are indexed. Regex extractions, calculated fields are search-time, improving indexing performance.
|
| 5 | D | top shows most common values with count/percent. stats count by field also creates frequency but top is optimized. rare shows least
common. |
| 6 | B | Sourcetype drives props.conf: TIME_FORMAT, SHOULD_LINEMERGE, reporting. Critical for proper CIM normalization. |
| 7 | C | Indexer parses the data stream into events, writes to buckets. Search head searches indexers. |
| 8 | C | Earliest modifier correctly limits to 24h. Option B uses wildcard index which is inefficient. Best practice: sourcetype=linux_secure "failed"
earliest=-24h. |
| 9 | B | Indexer storage lifecycle: hot (writable), warm (rolled from hot), cold, frozen/thawed/deletion via indexes.conf settings. |
| 10 | B | Splunkbase apps: e.g., Splunk Enterprise Security, ITSI, Add-on for Unix/Linux, AWS TA which maps to CIM for correlation. |

|---|--------|-------------|

| 24 | B | Classic anti-pattern: misconfigured TA sending _internal to main. Check inputs.conf _internal, indexes.conf _internal not forwarding,

| 25 | B | SmartStore: indexes.conf [cachemanager] and [index:main] remotePath = s3://..., repFactor=auto, homePath local cache. Dramatically
reduces local storage need, essential for large Cloud & Classic clusters 2024+. |
| 26 | B | Ingest Processor / DSP / Edge Processor is Splunk's major 2024-2026 feature to reduce ingest cost: SPL2 pipelines to where NOT
status=200 style filtering, hashing/masking PII before index, routing to S3 datalake. Critical for SIV management. |
| 27 | B | Needs hardening as ES Correlation/Search. Add threshold tuning, per-user analysis, risk scoring eval risk=..., notable generation. Use
timechart for visualization. Best practice: CIM map errorCode. Alternative: streamstats for sliding threshold. |

| 28 | B | Enterprise security: Use Deployment Server / Deployment Manager, Client certificate management, splunk.secret, Ingest Manager for

| 29 | B | Critical architecture distinctions tested on Architect exam: Summary (collect/sistats), Metrics (mcatalog, mstats), Acceleration

# Active Directory & LDAP - Certification Exam
**Course:** Active Directory & LDAP Administration and Troubleshooting
**Level:** Beginner to Advanced | **Total Questions:** 30 | **Updated:** 2024-2026 Standards (Windows Server 2022/2025, LDAP v3, LDAPS,
Entra ID Connect context)
**Passing Score:** 70% | **Time Limit:** 90 Minutes
**Instructions:** Each question has 4 options (A-D). Choose the best answer.
---
Part 1: Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
**Q1. What does LDAP stand for?**
A) Lightweight Directory Access Protocol
B) Logical Directory Authentication Protocol
C) Layered Directory Access Process
D) Local Domain Access Protocol
**Q2. In Active Directory, what is the primary purpose of a Domain Controller (DC)?**
A) To provide internet access to clients
B) To store user accounts, authenticate users, and enforce security policies
C) To act only as a DNS cache server
D) To manage hardware drivers
**Q3. What is the default LDAP port for unencrypted communication and the default port for LDAPS (LDAP over SSL/TLS)?**
A) 389 for LDAP, 636 for LDAPS
B) 88 for LDAP, 389 for LDAPS
C) 443 for LDAP, 389 for LDAPS
D) 3268 for LDAP, 3269 for LDAPS
**Q4. Which naming format is used to represent an object uniquely in LDAP, e.g., CN=John Doe,OU=Users,DC=example,DC=com?**
A) UPN (User Principal Name)
B) DN (Distinguished Name)
C) RDN (Relative Distinguished Name) only
D) GUID
**Q5. What are the three main partitions that exist in every Active Directory forest?**
A) Domain, Configuration, and Schema partitions
B) Users, Computers, and Groups partitions
C) Forest, Tree, and Domain partitions
D) Public, Private, and System partitions
**Q6. Which Active Directory object type is used to logically group users, computers, and other objects for delegation and Group Policy
application?**
A) Organizational Unit (OU)
B) Security Group
C) Container
D) Site
**Q7. What is the function of the Global Catalog (GC) in Active Directory?**
A) It only stores Group Policies
B) It provides a searchable partial copy of all objects in the forest and enables forest-wide searches and logon UPN resolution
C) It is a backup of SYSVOL
D) It manages DHCP leases
**Q8. Which authentication protocol is the default in modern Active Directory (2024-2026) for domain authentication?**
A) NTLMv1
B) Kerberos v5
C) PAP
D) CHAP
**Q9. What does the acronym FSMO stand for in AD, and which operation master is forest-wide?**
A) Flexible Single Master Operation - Schema Master is forest-wide
B) Fixed System Management Object - PDC Emulator is forest-wide
C) File System Master Operator - RID Master is forest-wide
D) Federated Single Management Operation - Infrastructure Master is forest-wide
**Q10. Which tool/command is most commonly used in Windows Server 2022/2025 to manage Active Directory users and computers via GUI?**

--- PAGE 155 ---
A) dsa.msc / Active Directory Users and Computers (ADUC)
B) dnsmgmt.msc
C) regedit.exe
D) lusrmgr.msc for domain accounts
Answer Key - Beginner
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 1 | A | LDAP = Lightweight Directory Access Protocol, an open standard application protocol for directory services. |
| 2 | B | DCs host AD DS database (ntds.dit), authenticate, authorize, replicate, and enforce policies. |
| 3 | A | IANA assigned 389/tcp for ldap, 636/tcp for ldaps. 3268/3269 are Global Catalog ports. |
| 4 | B | DN is the full path. RDN is the relative part (e.g., CN=John Doe). |
| 5 | A | Every AD forest has Domain, Configuration, and Schema application directory partitions. DomainDNSZones and ForestDNSZones are
additional if DNS is AD-integrated. |
| 6 | A | OU is the primary object for delegation and GPO linking. Containers cannot have GPOs linked directly. |
| 7 | B | GC holds partial attribute set (PAS) of every object in the forest, essential for forest searches and universal group caching. |
| 8 | B | Kerberos v5 is default since Windows 2000. NTLM is fallback only. |
| 9 | A | FSMO = Flexible Single Master Operations. Schema Master and Domain Naming Master are forest-wide; RID, PDCe, Infrastructure are
domain-wide. |
| 10 | A | ADUC (dsa.msc) is the standard MMC for managing users/computers/groups/OUs. |

Part 2: Intermediate Level (Questions 11-20)
**Q11. A user reports slow logons at a branch office. You suspect LDAP referral or site configuration issues. Which AD component should you
check?**
A) AD Sites and Services - Subnet to Site mapping and Site Links cost/replication schedule

**Q12. What is the difference between LDAP simple bind and SASL bind, in context of AD hardening in 2024-2026 best practices?**
A) Both are equally secure
B) Simple bind sends credentials in cleartext unless protected by TLS; SASL (e.g., GSSAPI with Kerberos) provides negotiated authentication and
is required after Microsoft’s LDAP channel binding and signing hardening updates

D) (!(userAccountControl=*))
**Q14. In AD replication, what is the main difference between intra-site and inter-site replication in Server 2022/2025 defaults?**

B) Both are identical
C) Inter-site is always faster

**Q15. You have to enforce LDAPS for a third-party app binding to AD. What are the requirements for Domain Controllers as of 2024?**

B) Each DC needs a valid Computer certificate with Server Authentication EKU, with FQDN, issued by trusted CA, and certificate supports
Schannel; client must trust CA chain and connect via port 636/3269 with TLS 1.2+

D) LDAPS works on port 389 automatically
**Q16. What is the purpose of AD DS tombstone lifetime and Deleted Objects container?**
A) To permanently delete objects instantly

**Q18. In an LDAP URL, what does “OU=Sales,DC=corp,DC=example,DC=com??sub?(department=Marketing)” represent?**
A) Base DN is OU=Sales,DC=corp,DC=example,DC=com, scope is subtree (sub), filter is (department=Marketing)
B) It is an LDIF entry
C) It is a DN only
D) It is a Kerberos ticket
**Q19. What is the SYSVOL and what replication technology is used for it in current supported OS (Windows Server 2016+ forest)?**
A) SYSVOL stores logon scripts and Group Policy templates; replication uses DFSR (Distributed File System Replication) - FRS is deprecated
and unsupported
B) SYSVOL stores user passwords; uses FRS
C) SYSVOL is the AD database file
D) SYSVOL stores DNS zones only
**Q20. A company merges and needs to maintain user searches across two forests. Which approach avoids password sync and uses LDAP
referrals or forest trust?**
A) Configure a Forest Trust (transitive) with DNS conditional forwarders and allow selective authentication; or use LDAP referrals to query GC port
3268
B) Just copy all users manually
C) Disable Kerberos
D) Use workgroup mode
Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | A | Correct Site/Subnet ensures clients discover nearest DC/GC via DCLocator. Missing subnets cause random DC selection and slow
logon. |
| 12 | B | Microsoft security updates (ADV190023, 2020-2024 enforcement) require LDAP signing and channel binding. Simple bind must be over
LDAPS/StartTLS. SASL/GSSAPI uses Kerberos sealing. |
| 13 | A | userAccountControl bit 2 = ACCOUNTDISABLE. LDAP_MATCHING_RULE_BIT_AND (1.2.840.113556.1.4.803) checks bit flag. 512 =
normal enabled account. |
| 14 | A | KCC generates intra-site topology with 15 sec change notification. Inter-site compressed and scheduled; ISTG handles bridgeheads. |
| 15 | B | LDAPS requires valid cert on DC with Server Auth EKU, FQDN in SAN/CN, trusted chain. TLS 1.2+ enforced per 2022/2025 hardening.
Self-signed not recommended for prod. |
| 16 | B | Tombstone lifetime default 180 days lets deletions replicate. After expiry, object is gone; need AD Recycle Bin for recovery. |
| 17 | A | Import-Module ActiveDirectory; Import-Csv users.csv | New-ADUser - verbose is current best practice. Use -Path, -AccountPassword etc.
|
| 18 | A | LDAP URL format: ldap://host/base_dn?attributes?scope?filter. ??sub? means subtree search. |
| 19 | A | SYSVOL = \domain\SYSVOL share. DFSR replaces FRS since Server 2008 R2 migration; FRS removed in Server 2022/2025. |
| 20 | A | Forest Trust or External Trust with selective auth allows cross-forest Kerberos/LDAP queries. GC port 3268 allows forest-wide read. No
password sync needed unlike Entra Connect. |

Part 3: Advanced Level (Questions 21-30)
**Q21. In Windows Server 2025, you are hardening LDAP with Channel Binding Token (CBT) and LDAP Signing. A legacy Java app using simple
bind fails after CVE-2017-8563 style hardening. What is the most secure compatible fix?**
A) Disable LDAP signing via registry LdapEnforceChannelBinding=0
B) Configure the app to use LDAPS on 636 with StartTLS, use SASL GSSAPI or simple bind over TLS 1.2+ with channel binding, and set registry
LdapEnforceChannelBinding to 2 (supported) and Require signing
C) Allow anonymous bind
D) Open port 389 without TLS to internet
**Q22. You have a forest with 3 domains. An attacker exploited ESC8 (AD CS HTTP enrollment relay). Which LDAP/S protection is critical to
prevent AD CS relay to LDAP/LDAPS?**
A) LDAPS alone stops relaying
B) Enforce LDAPS channel binding + Extended Protection for Authentication (EPA) on AD CS HTTP endpoints, enable LDAP signing, and disable
HTTP enrollment without EPA / enable Require SSL + AD CS auditing
C) Changing DC password
D) Enabling SMB signing only

--- PAGE 157 ---
**Q23. Advanced LDAP query optimization: You run (memberOf=CN=LargeGroup,OU=Groups,DC=corp,DC=com) and it is slow. Why and what is
the best practice fix on Server 2022+?**
A) memberOf is not indexed by default, it's a constructed backlink; use LDAP_MATCHING_RULE_IN_CHAIN:1.2.840.113556.1.4.1941: against
member DN or query tokenGroups/global catalog, or index member attribute forward link
B) Add more RAM only
C) Use filter (objectClass=*)
D) Restart DC
**Q24. During a DC DSRM recovery and AD database defrag scenario, you notice ntds.dit size bloat due to lingering objects. Which sequence
aligns with 2024 best practices?**
A) Use repadmin /removelingeringobjects or repadmin /replsingleobject with /h, verify Event ID 1388, enable Strict Replication Consistency
registry, and if needed perform offline defrag via ntdsutil only after semantic database analysis
B) Delete ntds.dit manually
C) Disable KCC
D) Set tombstone lifetime to 1 day
**Q25. You are designing LDAP authentication for Linux clients (SSSD) against AD with Kerberos. Which configuration provides secure, accurate,
and future-proof (2024-2026) integration?**
A) SSSD with id_provider=ad, auth_provider=ad, ldap_id_mapping=True, use sasl_gssapi + Kerberos, TLS with CA trust, enforce GSSAPI
signing/sealing, and use auto-discovery of sites
B) Plain LDAP simple bind with admin password in sssd.conf
C) Use LDAP anonymous
D) Disable DNS discovery
**Q26. In Active Directory Recycle Bin (enabled since 2008 R2) and newer 2025 Privileged Access Management, what is difference between
Deleted Objects vs Recycled Objects and msDS-DeletedObjectLifetime?**
A) When Recycle Bin enabled, deleted objects become logically deleted (isDeleted) and retain most attributes for Deleted Lifetime, then recycled;
msDS-DeletedObjectLifetime controls time in logically deleted state before conversion to tombstone, allowing authoritative restore with
Restore-ADObject
B) No difference
C) Recycle Bin deletes immediately
D) Deleted Objects are in SYSVOL
**Q27. A query against Global Catalog port 3268 fails to return the employeeNumber attribute. Why?**
A) GC only returns attributes in Partial Attribute Set (PAS); employeeNumber is not in PAS by default, must add to PAS via Schema or query a
specific domain DC on port 389/636
B) GC is broken
C) The attribute does not exist
D) Port 3268 only works for Kerberos
**Q28. You need to audit who modified OU ACLs and performed DCSync-like LDAP replication (DS-Replication-Get-Changes-All). Which AD
auditing and LDAP controls to enable?**
A) Enable Advanced Audit Policy: Directory Service Changes + DS Access auditing, SACLs on OU, Event 5136/4662, and monitor for control
access right 1131f6ad-... (DS-Replication-Get-Changes-All) / add to Protected Users, limit via AdminSDHolder, enable LDAP logging Field
Engineering
B) No auditing needed
C) Only enable Logon auditing
D) Disable Auditing
**Q29. You are implementing LDAP referral chasing for multi-domain forest with application expecting RFC 4511 compliance. Which behavior
should you implement?**
A) Follow referrals automatically up to limit, rebind with same credentials if same forest, respect ManageDsaIT control, and handle continuation
references with proper TLS; avoid chasing cross-forest to untrusted hosts to prevent credential leak - implement LDAP_OPT_REFERRALS on/off
per Microsoft KB
B) Always ignore referrals
C) Follow referrals to any external internet host
D) Use UDP for referrals
**Q30. Windows Server 2025 introduces improved Entra ID (Azure AD) integration and deprecates NTLM in some scenarios. For AD LDAP
hardening, what is the roadmap-aligned best practice for 2026?**
A) Keep NTLMv1 enabled
B) Disable NTLM where possible, enforce Kerberos AES 256, LDAP signing required, channel binding required, TLS 1.3 for LDAPS, Credential
Guard, Protected Users group, and move legacy LDAP apps to LDAPS or Entra ID LDAP via Entra Domain Services with managed identities
C) Use LM Hash
D) Disable Kerberos
Answer Key - Advanced

--- PAGE 158 ---
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | Microsoft LdapEnforceChannelBinding=2 + LDAP signing enforcement. Apps must move to TLS + CBT. Disabling is insecure
workaround violating CVE-2017-8563 fix. |
| 22 | B | ESC8 exploits AD CS HTTP enrollment without EPA allowing NTLM relay to LDAP/S web enroll. Fix: Enable EPA, Require SSL, disable
NTLM on CS web, enforce LDAPS Signing/Channel Binding (ADV190023) and LDAP Signing. Patch 2022-2024 KB5014754. |
| 23 | A | memberOf is constructed non-indexed. Use matching rule OID 1.2.840.113556.1.4.1941: (member:1.2.840.113556.1.4.1941:=DN) or
tokenGroups attribute (also constructed). Indexing forward link (member) improves performance. |
| 24 | A | Strict replication consistency (default since 2008+) prevents lingering objects replication. Event 1388 indicates lingering. Correct tool
repadmin /removelingeringobjects / legacy. Offline defrag ntdsutil requires DSRM downtime. |
| 25 | A | SSSD AD provider uses auto id mapping, GSSAPI sealed, DNS site discovery, realmd. Best practice: no cleartext passwords, validate
TLS cert, use Kerberos keytab. 2024 RHEL docs recommend. |
| 26 | A | With Recycle Bin, msDS-deletedObjectLifetime after window object becomes recycled object (stripped). Can restore with GUI or
PowerShell Restore-ADObject, preserving GUID, SID, group memberships (linked attributes restored). |
| 27 | A | GC PAS limited to ~200 attributes. Customize via Schema MMC > Attributes > check Replicate to GC. Causes schema replication. |
| 28 | A | DCSync requires extended right DS-Replication-Get-Changes-All. Monitoring via 4662 + properties. Enable Directory Service Access
Audit + LDAP Interface Events registry for diagnostics. Reduce privileged accounts. |
| 29 | A | RFC 4511 referrals. Security risk: credential forward to rogue host. Microsoft recommendation to manage referral chasing flag and
validate TLS for referral targets. |
| 30 | B | Microsoft NTLM deprecation roadmap 2024-2026 (Windows Server 2025/Win11 24H2). Enforce LDAP Signing + CBT, TLS 1.3, AES,
decommission LDAP simple on 389, use OAuth2/Entra for modern apps. |

800-171 LDAP hardening, and real-world troubleshooting 2024-2026. All ports, OIDs, and tools verified against current builds as of Dec 2025.

31_Cybersecurity.md
# Cybersecurity - Comprehensive Examination
**Course Code:** 31_Cybersecurity
**Total Questions:** 30 (10 Beginner, 10 Intermediate, 10 Advanced)
**Format:** Multiple Choice - 4 Options per Question
**Coverage:** 2024-2026 Threat Landscape, Frameworks, and Best Practices
---
Part A: Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
Q1. What does the CIA triad in cybersecurity stand for?
A) Confidentiality, Integrity, Availability
B) Control, Inspection, Analysis
C) Cyber, Internet, Access
D) Central, Intelligence, Agency
Q2. Which of the following is considered the most common initial attack vector in
2024-2025 breach reports (Verizon DBIR)?
A) Physical theft of devices
B) Phishing and social engineering
C) SQL injection
D) Bluetooth exploits
Q3. What is the primary purpose of Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA)?
A) To make passwords longer
B) To require two or more verification methods to reduce account takeover risk
C) To encrypt hard drives
D) To block all external traffic
Q4. In 2024-2026, which type of malware encrypts victim data and demands payment?
A) Spyware
B) Adware
C) Ransomware
D) Rootkit
Q5. Which protocol ensures secure browsing by encrypting HTTP traffic?
A) FTP
B) HTTPS (HTTP over TLS)
C) SMTP
D) Telnet
Q6. What is a firewall's basic function?
A) To scan for viruses in emails
B) To filter network traffic based on security rules and block unauthorized access
C) To backup data automatically
D) To create strong passwords
Q7. What does GDPR primarily regulate as of 2024-2026 enforcement?
A) Cryptocurrency mining
B) Protection and privacy of personal data of EU citizens
C) Hardware manufacturing standards
D) Internet speed requirements
Q8. Which of these is a strong password according to NIST SP 800-63B 2024 guidelines?
A) P@ssw0rd!
B) 12345678

--- PAGE 160 ---
C) A long, unique passphrase like "correct-orbit-cloud-telescope-94!"
D) Your birthdate
Q9. What is phishing?
A) A method to improve network speed
B) A fraudulent attempt to obtain sensitive information by impersonating a trustworthy entity
C) A type of firewall configuration
D) A data backup technique
Q10. What should you do if you receive a suspicious email with an unexpected attachment
in 2025?
A) Open it immediately to check what it is
B) Forward it to all colleagues
C) Do not open it, report it to IT/Security team via official phish reporting button, and delete it
D) Reply asking if it's legitimate
**Answer Key - Beginner Level:**
1. A - Confidentiality, Integrity, Availability are the three core security principles.
2. B - Verizon DBIR 2024/2025 continues to show phishing/social engineering as top initial access, involved in ~68% of breaches.
3. B - MFA requires multiple factors (something you know, have, are) - Microsoft reports it blocks 99.2% of account takeover attacks.
4. C - Ransomware encrypts data for extortion; 2024-2025 saw rise of RaaS groups like LockBit, BlackCat/ALPHV, and Cl0p.
5. B - HTTPS uses TLS to encrypt web traffic.
6. B - Firewall filters inbound/outbound traffic per ruleset.
7. B - GDPR regulates personal data protection and privacy for EU residents, with fines up to 4% global turnover.
8. C - NIST SP 800-63B (2024 update) recommends long, memorable, unique passphrases over complex short passwords and against periodic
forced rotation.
9. B - Phishing impersonates trusted entities to steal credentials/data.
10. C - Standard 2024-2026 SOC playbook: don't open, report via official channel, delete. Use phishing report button in Outlook/Gmail.
---

Q11. What is the difference between vulnerability, threat, and risk according to NIST?
A) They are all the same
B) Vulnerability is a weakness, threat is a potential actor/method to exploit it, risk is the potential impact/likelihood of exploitation

D) Risk only applies to physical security

D) ITIL v3

D) A password manager

A) Disabling email entirely
