Q15. What is Zero Trust Architecture (ZTA) as defined in NIST SP 800-207 and CISA's 2024
Zero Trust Maturity Model?
A) Trusting all internal network traffic
B) Never trust, always verify - no implicit trust, continuous verification of users/devices regardless of location
C) Zero firewalls needed
D) Trusting only VPN users
Q16. Which type of attack exploits CVE-2024-3400, CVE-2023-4966 (Citrix Bleed), and
MOVEit CVE-2023-34362 pattern?
A) Zero-day exploitation of edge/network appliances for initial access
B) Physical brute force
C) Insider typing errors
D) DNS poisoning only
Q17. What is the purpose of an Endpoint Detection and Response (EDR) / XDR solution in
2024-2026 SOC operations?
A) Only to block spam calls
B) To provide continuous monitoring, detection, investigation, and automated response to threats on endpoints and across domains
C) To increase CPU performance
D) To manage printer queues
Q18. What does CVSS 4.0 (released 2023-2024) measure?
A) Cost of software
B) Severity of security vulnerabilities with improved metrics for safety, automation, and threat intelligence
C) Network bandwidth
D) Employee satisfaction
Q19. Which encryption protocol replaced WPA2 as the recommended Wi-Fi security
standard by 2024-2025?
A) WEP
B) WPA3 (Wi-Fi Protected Access 3) with SAE handshake and forward secrecy
C) WPA1
D) No encryption
Q20. What is the correct incident response lifecycle order per NIST SP 800-61r2?
A) Eradication, Preparation, Detection, Recovery
B) Preparation, Detection & Analysis, Containment, Eradication, Recovery, Post-Incident Activity/Lessons Learned
C) Recovery first, then Preparation
D) Detection only
**Answer Key - Intermediate Level:**
11. B - Vulnerability = weakness/flaw, Threat = actor/circumstance that could exploit it, Risk = likelihood x impact.
12. A - NIST CSF 2.0 released Feb 26, 2024 expanded to all sectors with Govern function added. ISO 27001:2022 is current version, not 2013.
13. B - MITRE ATT&CK is a globally accessible knowledge base of adversary TTPs used for threat modeling and detection engineering.
14. B - BEC mitigation requires email auth (DMARC p=reject, SPF, DKIM), MFA resistant to phishing (FIDO2), and out-of-band verification for
financial transactions - CISA/FBI guidance 2024-2025.
15. B - ZTA eliminates implicit trust. CISA Zero Trust Maturity Model v2 (2024) emphasizes identity, devices, networks, data, applications.
16. A - 2024-2025 saw massive exploitation of edge devices: Palo Alto PAN-OS CVE-2024-3400, Citrix NetScaler CVE-2023-4966, MOVEit
Transfer CVE-2023-34362 - CISA KEV catalog pattern.
17. B - EDR/XDR provides telemetry, behavioral analysis, and response (isolation, kill process, rollback) - critical as EDR evasion rose in
2024-2025.
18. B - CVSS 4.0 released Oct 2023, adopted widely in 2024 with new supplemental metrics (Safety, Automatable, Recovery) and better OT/IoT
scoring.
19. B - WPA3 is now required for Wi-Fi 6/6E certification, provides SAE (Simultaneous Authentication of Equals) vs WPA2's PSK, protects against
offline dictionary attacks.
20. B - NIST SP 800-61r2 lifecycle is the industry standard IR process.

Q28. Under SEC Cyber Incident Disclosure Rules effective Dec 2023-2024 and NIS2 (EU)
effective Oct 2024, what is required?
A) No disclosure needed
B) SEC: US public companies must disclose material cyber incidents within 4 business days via 8-K Item 1.05; NIS2 requires 24h early warning,
72h notification, final report for essential entities
C) Disclose after 1 year
D) Only disclose ransomware
Q29. Which detection technique is most effective against fileless, LOLBIN (Living Off The
Land Binaries), and EDR evasion techniques seen in 2024-2026 (e.g., Volt Typhoon,
Scattered Spider)?
A) Signature-based AV only
B) Behavioral analytics, memory scanning, AMSI, kernel-level telemetry, and hunting for LOLBins (powershell.exe, mshta.exe, rundll32) with
parent-child anomaly detection and Zero Trust application allowlisting
C) Disabling PowerShell entirely
D) No detection possible
Q30. What is Post-Quantum Cryptography (PQC) and why is NIST PQC standardization
(FIPS 203, 204, 205 released Aug 2024) critical in 2024-2026?
A) Faster classical encryption
B) New algorithms (ML-KEM/Kyber for key encapsulation, ML-DSA/Dilithium and SLH-DSA/SPHINCS+ for signatures) designed to resist
decryption by future cryptographically relevant quantum computers - harvest now, decrypt later (HNDL) threat
C) Quantum computers are already breaking RSA
D) Only for government use
**Answer Key - Advanced Level:**
21. B - Modern RaaS (LockBit, BlackCat, Cl0p, Scattered Spider) exfiltrates before encryption, publishes on DLS (data leak sites), plus DDoS and
customer harassment to force payment. Average ransom demand $1.5M+ in 2024.
22. B - Passkeys use FIDO2/WebAuthn public-key crypto, origin binding prevents phishing, private key in secure enclave/TPM. CISA says
phishing-resistant MFA is #1 defense; Google reported 50% adoption in 2024 with 200M+ passkeys.
23. B - Microsoft MSRC ~70%, Google Project Zero/Chromium ~70% memory safety CVEs. CISA/NSA/FBI 2023-2024 memory safety roadmap
pushes Rust, CISA Secure by Design requires roadmap from manufacturers.
24. B - SASE (Gartner) architecture replaces VPN concentrators with ZTNA - identity-aware, least-privilege per app, inspected at cloud PoP. VPN
grants network access; ZTNA grants app-level access after continuous trust assessment.
25. B - Supply chain compromises poison trusted vendor. 3CX compromised via X_Trader (2023), MOVEit via 0-day, SolarWinds Orion via
SUNBURST backdoor - detected by anomalous SAML token abuse and build pipeline compromise.
26. B - Cloud breaches 2024: 82% due to misconfig (open S3 buckets, public EBS snapshots), overprivileged IAM, leaked secrets in GitHub.
CSPM/CIEM tools enforce least privilege, MFA for IAM, guardrails.
27. B - OWASP LLM01:2023/2025 Prompt Injection. Indirect injection: malicious instructions hidden in document/email/web that LLM plugin reads
and executes. Mitigation: input validation, sandboxing, LLM guardrails, human-in-the-loop for actions.
28. B - SEC rule 33-11216 effective Dec 18, 2023 requires 4-business-day material incident disclosure, no unreasonable delay; NIS2 (Directive
EU 2022/2555) effective Oct 17 2024 expands scope and enforces 24/72h reporting to CSIRTs and executive accountability.
29. B - Fileless attacks abuse LOLBins and fileless execution (in-memory .NET, Cobalt Strike). Modern EDR/XDR uses kernel callbacks
(PsSetCreateProcessNotify), AMSI for script scanning, behavioral ML, and MITRE ATT&CK T1218/T1059 detections. Volt Typhoon (China) used
LOLBins for 5+ years undetected.
30. B - FIPS 203 ML-KEM (Kyber), FIPS 204 ML-DSA (Dilithium), FIPS 205 SLH-DSA (SPHINCS+). NIST finalized Aug 13, 2024. HNDL:
adversaries collecting encrypted traffic now to decrypt later with CRQC (~2030-2035 estimate). Migration required by CNSA 2.0 by 2030-2033.

# HashiCorp Vault Associate & Operations - Certification Exam
**Exam Code: 32_Vault | Version: 2024-2026 | 30 Questions**
This exam covers HashiCorp Vault OSS and Enterprise, HCP Vault Dedicated, Vault 1.13 - 1.19+ features, Integrated Storage (Raft), sealing,
authentication, policies, and secret engines.

Part 1: Beginner Level (10 Questions)

Answer Key - Beginner
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q1 | B | Vault is secrets management, identity-based access with encryption as a service, dynamic credentials. |
| Q2 | B | Vault starts sealed after restart. Needs threshold of unseal keys (Shamir) or auto-unseal via cloud KMS/HSM. |
| Q3 | B | vault operator init initializes, returns unseal keys and initial root token. Should be done once per cluster. |
| Q4 | B | KV v2 adds versioning, delete vs destroy, metadata, CAS. Path becomes secret/data/ and secret/metadata/. |
| Q5 | B | KV v2 data path requires read capability. Listing versions requires list on secret/metadata/*. |
| Q6 | B | All tokens have TTL (default 32d for service tokens in recent Vault). Renewable if policy allows, limited by max TTL. |
| Q7 | B | vault login authenticates. Since Vault 1.13+, vault login -method=... is standardized. |
| Q8 | A | When sealed, Vault shows Seal Type (shamir, awskms, etc), Sealed true, Cluster Name, Storage, and is inactive for most ops. |
| Q9 | B | AppRole with SecretID delivery via response-wrapping, or trusted platform auth (k8s, aws, azure, jwt) are secure patterns. Root token is
anti-pattern. |
| Q10 | B | Dynamic secrets are leased: limited TTL, renewable, auto-revoked on expiration/revoke, reducing credential sprawl. |

| Q11 | A | vault kv undelete -versions=2 secret/my-secret via secret/undelete/my-secret. destroy cryptographically erases version history after

values in audit logs. Multiple audit devices allowed. |

Part 3: Advanced Level (10 Questions)
Q21. In Vault Enterprise 1.15+ with Auto-Unseal using AWS KMS, what is Seal Wrapping
and why is it relevant?
A) Auto-unseal doesn't need wrapping
B) Seal wrapping allows Vault to value-wrap critical values (like unseal recovery keys, root token during generation, Raft snapshots) using seal via
seal_wrap=true on mount points or via sealwrap endpoints, adding extra KMS/HSM encryption layer for temporary values in memory
C) It's a UI theme
D) Seal wrap encrypts the Vault binary
Q22. Performance Replication vs Disaster Recovery (DR) Replication in Vault Enterprise?
A) They are identical
B) Performance Replication: clusters active and serving reads/writes locally (eventual consistency, mounts filtered), enables low-latency; DR
Replication: secondary clusters are standby, no serving until promoted for DR, copies all data for failover
C) DR Replication allows writes on secondary
D) Performance replication is for OSS
Q23. You run Vault PKI as intermediate CA issuing short-lived certs. What critical
configuration for production as of Vault 1.17+ (with new PKI storage model)?
A) pki/root for leaf certs directly
B) Build hierarchy: Root CA (offline, maybe external) -> Intermediate mount -> Issuer with issuer_name, issuing_certificates, crlDistributionPoints,
auto-tidy, cross-cluster issuer sync with /pki/root/replace and managed issuers API; configure default_issuer, and use ACME or EST if needed
C) PKI does not support intermediate
D) Use KV to store certs
Q24. What does Vault's Control Groups and Sentinel (Enterprise) provide compared to
MFA (available since Vault 1.16+ in OSS)?
A) Same as policy
B) Control Groups: dual-authorization workflow (one user requests access, n approvers via identity, with authorizer and factor for time-delay);
Sentinel EGP/RGP for policy-as-code with conditions; Since Vault 1.16, OSS MFA now supports login MFA (TOTP, Okta, Duo, PingID) via mfa
method, enforced on auth
C) Sentinel is replacement for audit
D) MFA is Enterprise only
Q25. Vault Integrated Storage snapshots and disaster recovery best practice (Vault
1.18+)?
A) Snapshots contain plaintext secrets
B) vault operator raft snapshot save/restore produces encrypted snapshot of Raft data. Should enable auto-snapshots to S3/Azure/GCS via
storage "raft" { snapshot... } config, use snapshot_age to prevent restoring stale data, encrypt with seal wrapping
C) Snapshots are not supported with Raft
D) Snapshot restore can be done while Vault is unsealed and active

--- PAGE 168 ---
Q26. For the Transform secrets engine (Enterprise), what is Tokenization vs FPE?
A) They are same
B) Transform provides Format-Preserving Encryption (FPE: keeps format, e.g., CCN retains 16 digits) and tokenization (non-reversible mapping
using internal storage, with allowed_roles and stores), both support alphabet/templates, masking, tunable pseudonymization - critical for PCI
compliance, introduced 1.8+ and enhanced 2024
C) Transform is OSS
D) Transform cannot decrypt
Q27. Vault 1.15+ changed service token vs batch token design. Which statement is correct
for high-scale 2024 patterns?
A) Service tokens are stateless
B) Batch tokens are lightweight, stateless, encrypted blobs (no storage, no renewal, no lease, 32k size limit), ideal for high-throughput ephemeral
workloads (Vault Agent); Service tokens are persisted, heavy, renewable, can create children, support policies and entity
C) Batch tokens support renewal
D) Both types require storage
Q28. Troubleshooting Vault seal / HA failover: Node stays sealed after auto-unseal KMS
change and shows stored keys supported but not.... Fix?
A) Delete data
B) Check KMS key permissions, KMS key ID matches, seal stanza kms_seal config, and run vault operator unseal -migrate or vault operator
rekey -target=recovery when migrating seal types, ensure recovery keys present. From 1.16+, managed recovery keys via API. Also check seal
"awskms" stanza and IAM.
C) Ignore
D) Restart Vault without config
Q29. Vault Entropy Augmentation in Enterprise for compliance (FIPS 140-2/3)?
A) Not available
B) Enable entropy augmentation via seal "pkcs11" HSM + entropy "seal" and entropy_augmentation = true to source entropy from seal/HSM for
key generation (Transit, PKI), ensuring compliance; Requires HSM-backed seals, used since Vault 1.12+ enhanced
C) Entropy augmentation is OSS
D) It slows down Vault intentionally without purpose
Q30. In HCP Vault Dedicated (2024-2026), what is NOT true compared to self-managed
Vault?
A) HCP Vault Dedicated manages Raft storage, upgrades, backups, metrics automatically; you configure via HCP portal/Terraform, use
PrivateLink/Peering, namespaces; some features like custom plugins binaries restricted, seal is managed, root token generation via HCP UI
B) HCP Vault Dedicated still lets you own policies, auth, secrets engines via API same as OSS
C) HCP Vault is serverless with infinite TTL free
D) HCP Vault Dedicated still requires client to configure replication and performance tuning via config, but HashiCorp manages infrastructure
SLAs

|---|--------|-------------|
| Q21 | B | Seal wrapping via KMS/HSM wraps critical security params in transit. Enable via seal_wrap=true on listener/mount to encrypt

| Q22 | B | Perf replication (active-active locality, filterable paths per mount_secondary), DR replication (active-standby, warm standby, promote

OCSP, tidy auto. Intermediate should be online, root offline. |
| Q24 | B | Control Groups: control_group { factor "authorizer" { identity { group_names = ["managers"] } } } in policy. MFA login enforcement via
sys/mfa/method and identity/mfa/login-enforcement since 1.16 OSS. Sentinel EGP/RGP still Enterprise. |
| Q25 | B | Raft snapshots: save includes entire FSM, restore requires sealed Vault. Auto snapshots config in Vault 1.15+: raft {
snapshot_threshold, trailing_logs...} + autopilot. Snapshots encrypted if seal wrapping. For DR, store offsite. |
| Q26 | B | Transform: FPE preserves format/length for legacy apps. Tokenization maps token->plaintext in external store (Postgres). Supports
transformation templates, tokenization stores, tokenization max_ttl. Enterprise only, PCI use-case. |

--- PAGE 169 ---
| Q27 | B | Batch tokens = no Entity, no policy refresh, no children, no persistence (scalable to 100k RPS). Service tokens = full features. Use
batch for L7 sidecar token injection. Vault 1.19 documents improvements. |
| Q28 | B | Seal migration requires seal "transit" vs awskms changes. Recovery keys change: need migration path. vault operator unseal -migrate
or recovery rekey. Check IAM kms:Decrypt, Encrypt, DescribeKey. Logs: storage.raft and core: unseal. |
| Q29 | B | entropy "seal" { mode = "augmentation" } + entropy_augmentation = true in config. Sources entropy from HSM/TRNG for FIPS 140-2 L3
/ 140-3 compliance, required for regulated key generation. |
| Q30 | C | HCP Vault Dedicated is managed but NOT serverless unlimited: you pay for CU, storage, still handle policy design, auth. Vault still bills
per cluster hour. Manages HA, upgrades, snapshots. Restriction: no custom plugins uploading, limited sys endpoints. |

# Playwright & Cypress - Complete 30-Question Exam

C) Defines screenshot output

D) To intercept file system operations

--- PAGE 171 ---
Q9. Which assertion library does Playwright's expect use as its foundation?

Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 1 | A | Cypress runs IN the browser's same run-loop; Playwright controls browsers externally via CDP/WebSocket, enabling multi-tab,
multi-origin, multiple browsers. |
| 2 | B | npm init playwright@latest scaffolds project, installs browsers, creates config and example tests. |
| 3 | B | Since Cypress 10+, config is cypress.config.js/ts with ESM. cypress.json was deprecated. |
| 4 | B | Playwright locators are strict with auto-waiting, actionability checks and retry-ability built-in. |
| 5 | B | cy.get('[data-testid="login"]') is native; cy.findByTestId requires Testing Library plugin. |
| 6 | B | Playwright ships patched Chromium, Firefox, WebKit binaries, abstracted under same API. |
| 7 | A | testDir tells test runner where to look for *.spec.ts files. |
| 8 | A | cy.intercept() mocks/spies network, crucial for stubbing APIs, waiting on routes. |
| 9 | B | Playwright bundles Jest-like expect extended with async web matchers. |
| 10 | D | --headed runs headed, --ui launches Playwright's UI Mode (2023+). Both headed variations are valid. |

Answer Key - Intermediate Level
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | Docs since 2022 recommend locator over elementHandle. Locator re-evaluates, prevents stale element errors. |
| 12 | B | Modern: click auto-waits navigation. For explicit assertions use waitForURL or waitForLoadState. Promise.all pattern was v1 legacy. |
| 13 | B | Cypress commands are not Promises; they queue. Must chain or use .then(). Using const assignment breaks chain. |
| 14 | A | expect(page).toHaveScreenshot() compares. Config options like threshold, maxDiffPixelRatio control tolerance. Works cross-browser. |
| 15 | B | frameLocator is recommended since Playwright 1.26+, retains locator semantics inside frame. |
| 16 | A | Component testing mounts component in browser runner, allows props, spies, full DOM testing isolated from backend. |
| 17 | B | request fixture or APIRequestContext allows API testing within same Playwright project, sharing storageState etc. |
| 18 | A | --shard=x/y is official for CI sharding (GitHub Actions matrix). workers is intra-machine parallelism. |
| 19 | B | Cypress 12 introduced addQuery for retryable custom queries. Otherwise should() automatically retries until pass/timeout. |
| 20 | B | Trace Viewer is Playwright's killer debugging feature: full replay with actionability, network, DOM. |

Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | A | BrowserContext is lightweight isolated session. Playwright creates new context per test worker for true isolation, enabling parallel tests +
auth reuse. |
| 22 | B | Official docs recommend auth setup project, not UI login per test. storageState persists cookies/localStorage. Reduces test time 90% and
flakiness. |
| 23 | A | cy.origin introduced in Cypress 12 to unlock cross-origin flows (OAuth, Stripe). Commands inside origin callback run in secondary origin

| 24 | B | retires + web-first assertions + UI Mode with flaky detection. HTML reporter marks flaky if passes after retry. |

| 26 | A | Playwright CT is experimental but uses Vite + browser. Cypress CT more mature. Both isolate components. Playwright leverages same
fixtures/plugins. |
| 27 | A | POM best practice: locator initialization in constructor, actions as methods, no page.waitForTimeout, use getByRole/getByTestId,
reusable fixtures. |
| 28 | B | Playwright automatically traverses open shadow roots. Cypress default no shadow; must enable via option or .shadow() in v13+. |
| 29 | A | Dependencies feature (2023+) replaces globalSetup for many cases, provides visual dependency graph and teardown, better than plain
beforeAll. |
| 30 | A | Best practice 2024-2026: install with-deps, cache browsers, sharding + blob reporter merge, workers parallel, tracing only on retry for
performance. |

# Selenium & Test Automation - Certification Exam
**Course Code:** 34_Selenium
**Total Questions:** 30
**Duration:** 90 Minutes
**Passing Score:** 70%
**Selenium Version Covered:** Selenium 4.x (4.15 - 4.25+) | 2024-2026 Standards
---
Part 1: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
Q1. What is the primary purpose of Selenium WebDriver?
A) To test APIs and backend services
B) To automate and control web browsers for testing web applications
C) To manage database testing
D) To perform load testing on servers
Q2. Which of the following is the correct way to initialize ChromeDriver in Selenium 4
(2024+), where Selenium Manager is enabled by default?
A) System.setProperty("webdriver.chrome.driver", "/path/to/chromedriver"); WebDriver driver = new ChromeDriver();
B) WebDriver driver = new ChromeDriver(); // Selenium Manager handles driver binary automatically
C) ChromeDriver driver = ChromeDriver.getInstance();
D) WebDriver driver = DriverFactory.createChromeDriver();
Q3. Which locator strategy is considered the most preferred and robust in Selenium?
A) XPath with absolute path
B) ID
C) TagName
D) linkText with partial match
Q4. What does driver.get("https://example.com") do?

35_Rust.md
# Rust Programming - Comprehensive Examination
**Course Code:** 35_Rust
**Total Questions:** 30 (10 Beginner, 10 Intermediate, 10 Advanced)
**Format:** Multiple Choice (4 options per question)
**Coverage:** Accurate for Rust 1.70 - 1.85 (2024-2026 edition)
---
Part A: Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
Q1. What does Rust's ownership system guarantee at compile time without a garbage
collector?
A) Dynamic typing and runtime reflection
B) Memory safety and absence of data races
C) Automatic parallelization of all code
D) Unlimited mutable references
Q2. Which keyword is used to create an immutable variable binding in Rust?
A) var
B) let
C) const var
D) immutable
Q3. What will happen with this code? let mut x = 5; let y = &x; let z = &mut x;
A) It compiles fine, y and z can coexist
B) It fails to compile because you cannot have mutable and immutable borrows at the same time
C) It causes a runtime panic
D) y becomes mutable automatically
Q4. What is the default integer type in Rust if no type is annotated?
A) i32
B) i64
C) isize
D) i8
Q5. How do you define an enum with data in Rust?
A) enum Message { Quit, Text(String), Move { x: i32, y: i32 } }
B) type Message = Quit | Text(String)
C) class Message { Quit, Text }
D) enum Message : String {}
Q6. What does Option<T> represent?
A) A pointer that is always non-null
B) A type that can be either Some(T) or None, used to handle absence of value safely
C) A list of optional parameters
D) A deprecated feature replaced by null
Q7. Which macro is used to print to console with newline?
A) print!
B) println!
C) console.log!
D) echo!
Q8. What is Cargo in Rust ecosystem?
A) A garbage collector
B) Rust's official package manager and build system

--- PAGE 181 ---
C) A code formatter only
D) A virtual machine
Q9. What is the result of String vs &str?
A) They are identical
B) String is owned, heap-allocated, growable; &str is a borrowed string slice view
C) &str is owned and String is borrowed
D) String is only for ASCII
Q10. How does Rust handle errors idiomatically for recoverable errors?
A) Using try/catch exceptions
B) Returning Result<T, E> with variants Ok(T) and Err(E)
C) Returning null and checking null
D) Using setjmp/longjmp
---
Answer Key - Beginner Level
**Q1 Answer: B** - Rust's ownership/borrow checker guarantees memory safety (no use-after-free, double-free, null deref) and prevents data
races at compile time without GC.
**Q2 Answer: B** - let creates immutable binding by default; let mut makes it mutable. const is for compile-time constants.
**Q3 Answer: B** - Rust's borrowing rule: you can have either one mutable reference OR any number of immutable references, not both
simultaneously in same scope. Compiler error E0502.
**Q4 Answer: A** - Default integer type is i32 (32-bit signed), default float is f64.
**Q5 Answer: A** - Rust enums are algebraic data types and can hold data directly in variants, including tuple and struct variants.
**Q6 Answer: B** - Option eliminates null pointers; forces explicit handling of absence via pattern matching, unwrap, expect, ? etc.
**Q7 Answer: B** - println! prints with newline; print! without. Both are macros (!).
**Q8 Answer: B** - Cargo manages dependencies (crates.io), builds, runs, tests, benchmarks, and is the primary toolchain tool.
**Q9 Answer: B** - String is Vec<u8> wrapper owned; &str is fat pointer (ptr + len) to UTF-8 data. &String coerces to &str.
**Q10 Answer: B** - Recoverable errors use Result. Unrecoverable errors use panic!. Rust has no exceptions.
---
Part B: Intermediate Level (Questions 11-20)
Q11. What is the difference between Clone and Copy traits?
A) They are identical
B) Copy is implicit bitwise copy for stack-only types, Clone is explicit deep copy method .clone(); Copy requires Clone
C) Clone is implicit and Copy is explicit
D) Copy works only for heap types
Q12. Given fn foo(s: &mut String), what does lifetime elision mean here?
A) Lifetimes are ignored and memory leaks
B) Compiler assigns implicit lifetimes: fn foo<'a>(s: &'a mut String); output lifetime rules apply
C) You must always write lifetimes manually
D) It makes the reference static
Q13. What does this trait bound mean? fn bar<T: Display + Debug + Clone>(t: T)
A) T must implement at least one of these traits
B) T must implement all three traits simultaneously (via +)
C) It defines a new trait
D) It is invalid syntax
Q14. What is Box<dyn Trait> used for?
A) Stack allocation of trait
B) Heap-allocated trait object for dynamic dispatch when concrete type unknown at compile time
C) To avoid trait implementation

--- PAGE 182 ---
D) Compile-time generics only
Q15. What will tokio and async/await provide in modern Rust (2024+)?
A) Synchronous only execution
B) Asynchronous runtime with work-stealing scheduler; async fn returns Future, awaited without blocking thread
C) Automatic GC
D) Built-in HTTP server only
Q16. How does Arc<Mutex<T>> differ from Rc<RefCell<T>>?
A) Same, just naming
B) Arc is atomic reference counted thread-safe + Mutex thread-safe interior mutability; Rc+RefCell is single-threaded only
C) Arc<Mutex> is single-threaded
D) Rc is atomic
Q17. What is true about Rust's Iterator trait and adaptors like map, filter?
A) They are eager and allocate immediately
B) They are lazy: build chain, nothing executed until consumed by collect, for, sum etc.; zero-cost abstractions
C) They require GC
D) Iterators cannot be chained
Q18. Consider the code: let v = vec![1,2,3]; let closure = move || println!("{:?}", v); What
does move do?
A) Moves closure to another thread automatically
B) Forces closure to take ownership of captured variables (v), making it FnOnce if used
C) Copies v automatically
D) Makes closure async
Q19. What is a procedural macro in Rust?
A) A C-style macro with text substitution
B) A Rust function that takes TokenStream and returns TokenStream, executed at compile time to generate code (derive, attribute, function-like)
C) A runtime reflection API
D) Same as macro_rules!
Q20. How do you correctly handle lifetime in this struct: struct Foo<'a> { x: &'a str } Why
need 'a?
A) To make it static
B) To tell borrow checker that Foo cannot outlive the borrowed str data it holds
C) To allocate on heap
D) 'a is optional and can be omitted
---
Answer Key - Intermediate Level
**Q11 Answer: B** - Copy marker for types that can be duplicated by memcpy (i32, bool, char, &T). Clone can be expensive. If type is Copy, it is
also Clone. Assignment of Copy types copies, non-Copy moves.
**Q12 Answer: B** - Lifetime elision rules (3 rules) allow omitting lifetimes in many fn signatures. Compiler infers. For &mut single input, its lifetime
is assigned to all elided output lifetimes. Since 2024, Edition still respects these rules.
**Q13 Answer: B** - + means intersection bound. T must implement all listed. Alternatively where T: Display + Debug .
**Q14 Answer: B** - dyn Trait is trait object, size unknown at compile time (?Sized), so Box puts on heap and enables dynamic dispatch via vtable.
Alternatives: &dyn Trait, Arc<dyn Trait>.
**Q15 Answer: B** - Tokio is de facto async runtime. Rust 1.75+ stabilized async fn in traits. Futures are lazy, polling-based. Tokio's
multi-threaded scheduler is default since 1.0.
**Q16 Answer: B** - Arc: Atomic Reference Counted, thread-safe via atomics. Mutex: mutual exclusion with blocking. Rc: non-atomic, RefCell:
runtime borrow check single thread. For threads use Arc<Mutex> or Arc<RwLock>.
**Q17 Answer: B** - Iterator adaptors are lazy and often optimized to same as hand-written loops (zero-cost). Consuming adaptors trigger
execution.

--- PAGE 183 ---
**Q18 Answer: B** - move closures take ownership of environment. Without move, they borrow. Move closures are needed for thread::spawn and
when returning closures that outlive captured stack variables. Ownership affects Fn trait: FnOnce consumes, FnMut mutates, Fn immutably
borrows.
**Q19 Answer: B** - Proc macros are powerful compile-time code generation; defined in separate crate with proc-macro = true. Three types:
#[derive(Custom)], #[attribute], bang!(). Uses syn and quote typically.
**Q20 Answer: B** - Struct holding reference must have lifetime parameter to ensure reference validity. 'a is generic lifetime. Without it struct
would hold dangling ref. 'static would be overly restrictive.
---
Part C: Advanced Level (Questions 21-30)
Q21. What is the difference between Send and Sync marker traits?
A) They are same
B) Send: ownership can be transferred to another thread; Sync: reference &T can be shared between threads (i.e., T is thread-safe to share). Rc
is neither Send nor Sync.
C) Send means thread-safe sharing, Sync means transferring
D) Both auto-implemented for all types
Q22. Consider unsafe Rust: What is correct about unsafe block?
A) It disables all safety checks and makes Rust like C
B) It allows 5 specific unsafe operations (deref raw pointer, call unsafe fn, access/mutate static mut, implement unsafe trait, access union field) but
still upholds borrow checker for safe code inside; programmer must uphold invariants
C) It turns off borrow checker completely
D) Unsafe code cannot be wrapped in safe abstraction
Q23. What is Pin and why is it needed for async (2024)?
A) Pins memory to CPU core
B) Pin<P> guarantees data will not move in memory after being pinned, necessary because self-referential Futures generated by async/await
must not move after polling starts
C) It is for garbage collection
D) Pin is deprecated since 1.80
Q24. In Rust 1.81+, what is Result and ? operator desugaring for Option vs Result?
A) ? only works for Result
B) ? works for both: for Result it returns Err early on failure; for Option returns None early; desugars to match with From::from conversion for error
type
C) ? panics on Err
D) ? unwraps always
Q25. What are Generic Associated Types (GATs), stabilized in Rust 1.65?
A) Generics on functions only
B) Allows associated types in traits to themselves have generic parameters: e.g., trait LendingIterator { type Item<'a> where Self: 'a; fn
next<'a>(&'a mut self) -> Self::Item<'a>; } enabling lending/streaming iterators
C) Deprecated feature
D) Same as associated constants
Q26. What does std::mem::MaybeUninit<T> solve and how should it be used?
A) Initializes memory with zeros always
B) Allows handling of uninitialized memory safely without UB, replacing deprecated mem::uninitialized(). Must be initialized before assume_init();
use write(), MaybeUninit::new(), and assume_init_read
C) It is unsafe and should never be used
D) It automatically initializes on drop
Q27. Explain variance and PhantomData use case: struct MyBox<T> { ptr: *mut T,
_marker: PhantomData<T> }
A) Raw pointers have no variance; PhantomData tells compiler to treat MyBox as owning T, giving drop check and variance/covariance,
Send/Sync implications
B) PhantomData allocates T

--- PAGE 184 ---
C) It is only for documentation
D) Raw pointer already owns data
Q28. What is true about Rust's 2024 Edition and new features up to 1.85?
A) No changes since 2021 Edition
B) 2024 Edition (stabilized in Rust 1.85 Feb 2025) introduces: Reserve syntax for gen keyword, changes to unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn lint now
deny, &mut in let patterns, and RPIT lifetime capture rules for impl Trait more precise. Also stabilized: async fn in traits, impl Trait in associated
types, let else
C) It removes async/await
D) It adds GC
Q29. What is the memory layout and optimization for enum with #[repr(u8)] vs niche
optimization?
A) All enums are always 16 bytes
B) #[repr(u8)] forces discriminant to u8 and C-like layout; otherwise Rust can optimize Option<&T> and Option<NonZeroI32> to null-pointer /
niche optimization, using invalid bit patterns to avoid extra discriminant storage, making Option<Box<T>> same size as *mut T
C) Niche optimization is disabled since 1.70
D) Enums always have discriminant overhead
Q30. How would you implement a lock-free data structure with
std::sync::atomic::AtomicPtr and what ordering is needed?
A) Use Relaxed everywhere - always safe
B) Lock-free requires careful Ordering: Acquire for loads that see updates, Release for stores publishing, AcqRel for read-modify-write
(compare_exchange), often SeqCst for total order when needed. Must handle ABA problem, use AtomicPtr::compare_exchange, and ensure
reclamation (e.g., via crossbeam-epoch or hazard pointers) to avoid use-after-free UAF.
C) Atomics replace Mutex for all cases automatically
D) AtomicPtr cannot be used in Rust
---
Answer Key - Advanced Level
**Q21 Answer: B** - Send auto trait: type can be moved to another thread. Sync: &T is Send. Cell, Rc, *mut T are !Send !Sync. RefCell is Send
but not Sync? Actually RefCell is !Sync. Most types are Send+Sync. MutexGuard is !Send. Understanding essential for concurrency correctness.
**Q22 Answer: B** - unsafe doesn't turn off safety completely; only 5 operations allowed. Superpowers only inside unsafe. Safe Rust still checked.
Sound unsafe abstractions must ensure invariants: e.g., Vec uses unsafe internally but safe API.
**Q23 Answer: B** - Pinning prevents moves. Future may hold pointer to itself across await points. If moved after polling, self-reference invalid ->
UB. Box::pin, pin! macro (1.68), Pin::new_unchecked. Unpin marker for types safe to move after pin (most types).
**Q24 Answer: B** - ? uses From trait to convert error types automatically, enabling error propagation. Since early Rust, works for Option. Try trait
generalization ongoing, ? in 2024 works with more Try types.
**Q25 Answer: B** - GATs enable lending iterators (item borrows from self), generic associated types powering streaming iterators, family
patterns. Critical for async traits and collections.
**Q26 Answer: B** - MaybeUninit<T> is union of uninit bytes + T. Prevents UB from creating uninitialized instance. Correct pattern: let mut x =
MaybeUninit::uninit(); ptr.write(val); safe = x.assume_init().
**Q27 Answer: A** - *mut T has no ownership semantics, no drop check, invariant over T. PhantomData<T> makes compiler consider MyBox
owns T => covariance over T, requires T: drop check, auto traits affected. Also PhantomData<*mut T> vs PhantomData<T> different variance.
**Q28 Answer: B** - 2024 Edition released with Rust 1.85 (Feb 2025). Key: gen blocks reserved, unsafe {} required inside unsafe fn, new if let
chain improvements, RPITIT (Return Position Impl Trait In Trait) capture rules, let pattern ref mut changes. Includes async closure, etc.
**Q29 Answer: B** - Rust enum layout: default Rust repr allows optimization. Niche-filling means Option<&T> is same as pointer (null niche).
NonZero*, NonNull, bool etc all use niches. #[repr(C)] or u8 disables niche optimization for discriminant, gives predictable ABI.
**Q30 Answer: B** - Lock-free needs memory ordering reasoning. Relaxed insufficient for synchronization. Must use Acquire-Release to establish
happens-before. ABA solved via epoch-based reclamation (crossbeam crate is de facto). Rust's atomics map to C++20 model. Use
compare_exchange_weak in loop for LL/SC arch.
---
End of Examination
*Total: 30 Questions | Passing: 18 correct (60%)* | *Generated for Rust 1.70-1.85 (2024-2026)*

# SQL & Databases - Comprehensive Certification Exam

Q1. Which SQL statement is used to retrieve data from a database?

B) SELECT

D) PULL

A) HAVING
B) WHERE
C) FILTER
D) CONDITION
Q3. What does NULL represent in SQL?
A) Zero value
B) Empty string ''
C) Absence of value / unknown
D) False
Q4. Which of the following is the correct syntax to insert a new row into table users?

B) INSERT INTO users VALUES (...)
C) INSERT NEW users VALUES (...)
D) ADD INTO users VALUES (...)
Q5. Which aggregate function counts the number of rows?
A) SUM()
B) COUNT()
C) TOTAL()
D) NUMBER()
Q6. What is the default sort order of ORDER BY in SQL?
A) DESC
B) ASC
C) Random
D) No default, must specify
Q7. Which keyword is used to remove duplicate rows from a result set?
A) UNIQUE
B) DISTINCT
C) DEDUP

|---|--------|-------------|

| 5 | B | COUNT() counts rows; COUNT(*) includes NULLs, COUNT(col) excludes NULLs in that column. |
| 6 | B | ASC (ascending) is default if not specified. |
| 7 | B | DISTINCT removes duplicate rows from result set. |
| 8 | B | VARCHAR(MAX) in SQL Server and TEXT in PostgreSQL handle large text. CHAR is fixed length. |

Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 11 | B | INNER returns intersection; LEFT preserves all left rows with NULLs for no match on right. |

--- PAGE 188 ---
| 14 | A | ACID = Atomicity, Consistency, Isolation, Durability - core transaction properties. |

rank for ties. |
| 16 | B | CTE defined with WITH cte_name AS (SELECT ...) SELECT ... FROM cte_name. Improves readability and recursion. |
| 17 | C | NULL comparisons require IS NULL / IS NOT NULL because NULL = NULL is UNKNOWN. |
| 18 | B | UNION does DISTINCT sort to remove duplicates (slower); UNION ALL preserves all rows, faster, preferred when duplicates not an
issue or desired. |
| 19 | B | Classic ORM problem; solved with JOIN fetch, eager loading, or batching. |
| 20 | B | READ COMMITTED is default in Postgres and SQL Server (prevents dirty reads). REPEATABLE READ prevents non-repeatable reads
in Postgres, but phantom behavior varies. |

Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| 21 | B | DENSE_RANK with PARTITION BY dept_id ORDER BY salary DESC and filter rnk=2 correctly gets 2nd highest per dept, handling ties.
ROW_NUMBER could also work if unique second needed. |
| 22 | B | VACUUM is critical for MVCC bloat; MERGE added PG 15 (2022) merges insert/update/delete atomically, major feature 2024-2026. |
| 23 | B | Planner uses statistics; low selectivity => seq scan cheaper than index + random heap fetches. Solution: composite index, partial index,
or accept scan. |
| 24 | B | Accurate modern definition; Optimistic with version column is standard in Hibernate/EF Core 8/Prisma 5. |
| 25 | A | INCLUDE (SQL Server) and covering indexes in Postgres (INCLUDE since PG 11) enable Index-Only Scans, major performance
technique. |
| 26 | B | Deadlock victim selection and retry is standard. Prevention via ordered access is interview favorite. |
| 27 | B | Declarative partitioning is key for big data scalability, pruning is primary benefit. |
| 28 | B | Materialized views trade freshness for speed; CONCURRENTLY refresh avoids locking reads. |
| 29 | C | GIN is correct for @> containment. For mixed @> and expression on total, best is GIN + generated stored column + B-Tree, or GIN +
expression index. jsonb_path_ops more efficient. |

--- PAGE 190 ---
| 30 | A | Distributed SQL is CP, uses Raft/Paxos, provides strong consistency but pays latency/availability cost during partitions - essential
knowledge 2024-2026. |

# Course Title: MongoDB Certified Developer & Administrator - Comprehensive Exam
> Exam Code: 37_MongoDB | Valid for MongoDB 7.0 / 8.0 - 2024-2026 Curriculum | Atlas, mongosh, Vector Search, Queryable Encryption

Section 1: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
Q1. What is the fundamental storage format for documents in MongoDB?

mongosh (2024+)?

B) db.collection.insertOne()
C) db.collection.addOne()
D) db.collection.saveOne()
Q4. What does db.collection.find({ age: { $gt: 21 } }) do?
A) Finds documents where age is greater than 21
B) Finds documents where age is equal to 21
C) Deletes documents where age is greater than 21
D) Updates documents where age is greater than 21
Q5. Which tool is the official GUI for MongoDB as of 2024-2026?
A) Robo 3T
B) MongoDB Compass
C) NoSQL Booster
D) phpMyAdmin
Q6. What is a Collection in MongoDB analogous to in relational databases?
A) Database
B) Table
C) Row
D) Column
Q7. What is MongoDB Atlas?
A) A local file system storage engine
B) MongoDB's fully managed cloud Database-as-a-Service
C) A MongoDB query language
D) A legacy mongo shell
Q8. Which of the following is true about ObjectId?
A) It is 8 bytes long
B) It is 12 bytes including timestamp, random value, and counter
C) It is a string UUID only
D) It must be manually generated by the user always

--- PAGE 192 ---
Q9. How do you switch to or create a database named shop in mongosh?
A) SELECT shop
B) USE shop
C) use shop
D) db.switchTo('shop')
Q10. Which operator is used to match elements inside an array?
A) $in
B) $eq only
C) $and
D) $match
---
Answer Key - Beginner
1. B - MongoDB stores documents on disk and over the wire as BSON, a binary-encoded superset of JSON supporting additional types like
ObjectId, Date, Binary.
2. B - If not supplied, MongoDB adds _id: ObjectId() as unique primary key.
3. B - insertOne() and insertMany() are current methods. Legacy insert() is deprecated since MongoDB 5.0+.
4. A - $gt is greater than comparison query operator.
5. B - MongoDB Compass is the official GUI, supporting CRUD, index management, aggregation builder, and since 2023, Atlas Vector Search
visualization.
6. B - Collection ≈ Table, Document ≈ Row, Field ≈ Column.
7. B - Atlas is the multi-cloud managed platform for MongoDB, offering automated backups, scaling, Search, Vector Search, etc.
8. B - ObjectId is 12 bytes: 4-byte timestamp, 5-byte random value, 3-byte incrementing counter.
9. C - use shop in mongosh switches context; database is created lazily on first data write.
10. A - $in matches any value in an array of possibilities, e.g. { status: { $in: ['A','B'] } }.
---

---
Answer Key - Intermediate
11. B - Atlas Vector Search requires a dedicated Atlas Search index of type vectorSearch with fields: [{ type: 'vector', path: 'embedding',
numDimensions: 1536, similarity: 'cosine' }]. Regular indexes do not support ANN.
12. B - Best practice: $match early to reduce documents, then $group, then $sort. Order matters for performance.
13. B - oplog (operations log) is a capped collection in local db that all secondaries tail to replicate writes.
14. C - w: 'majority' ensures write propagated to majority of voting members, durable. In MongoDB 5.0+, default is majority.
15. B - Sharding distributes data across shard (mongod) nodes. Choice of shard key is critical for even distribution and avoiding jumbo chunks
(auto-merge improved in 7.0/8.0).
16. B - Queryable Encryption (QE) is evolution of CSFLE allowing equality queries on encrypted fields with server never seeing plaintext. GA in
MongoDB 7.0 (2023).
17. A - TTL index on a Date field: createIndex({ createdAt: 1 }, { expireAfterSeconds: 3600 }) auto-removes docs via background thread.
18. B - Since MongoDB 4.2, distributed multi-document ACID transactions supported on sharded clusters. Uses WiredTiger snapshot isolation.
19. A - createIndex({ field: 1 }) for ascending, -1 descending. Order in compound index affects sort and ESR rule.
20. A - MongoDB Atlas allows $search (BM25 text/atlas search) and $vectorSearch (ANN vector search) stages. MongoDB 8.0+ supports hybrid
search with Reciprocal Rank Fusion.

---

B) Shard key can never be changed

--- PAGE 195 ---
C) They handle only authentication
D) They are optional in production
Q29. Which best practice prevents a hot shard / jumbo chunk scenario in MongoDB
sharding?
A) Use monotonically increasing shard key like ObjectId or timestamp without hashed or ranged optimization
B) Use a high-cardinality, non-monotonic, well-distributed shard key (e.g., hashed shard key or compound key with hashed prefix) and enable
balancer + auto-split improved in 7.0+
C) Use single shard for all data
D) Disable balancer permanently
Q30. In Queryable Encryption, where does encryption/decryption happen and what does
the server see?
A) Encryption on server, server sees plaintext
B) Encryption and decryption happen exclusively on client driver with automatic encryption, server only sees encrypted documents and encrypted
index, never plaintext keys
C) Encryption on network layer only
D) Server does both
---
Answer Key - Advanced
21. B - WiredTiger uses document-level concurrency with MVCC, snapshots, and write-ahead log (journal). Locks at document level optimize
concurrent writes vs old collection-level MMAPv1.
22. B - Change streams use db.collection.watch() aggregation stage, tail oplog invisibly, resumable via resume token and startAfter. Supports pre-
and post-images since MongoDB 6.0.
23. A - Time series collections require timeseries: { timeField, metaField, granularity }. They use columnar storage, automatic bucketing, optimized
indexes. Improved compression in 7.0/8.0.
24. A - explain('executionStats') shows queryPlanner.winningPlan, executionTimeMillis, totalDocsExamined vs nReturned, index usage. Use with
allPlansExecution for rejected plans.
25. B - Aggregation memory limit 100 MB per stage. Since 6.0, spill-to-disk requires allowDiskUse: true, otherwise error code 292.
db.collection.find().sort() also has 100MB limit unless indexed.
26. A - MongoDB 5.0+ introduced resharding (sh.reshardCollection()) allowing change of shard key via zero-downtime resharding. MongoDB 6.0+
added refineCollectionShardKey to add suffix to shard key.
27. D - Atlas Vector Search officially supports cosine, euclidean, dotProduct (as of 2024-2026). manhattanGrid is not a supported similarity type.
28. B - Config servers run as CSRS (Config Server Replica Set) storing cluster metadata. Since 3.4 must be replica set; availability critical.
Mongos routers cache metadata and refresh from config servers.
29. B - Best practice: high cardinality, non-monotonic, query-isolated shard key. Hashed shard keys provide even distribution. Monotonic keys
cause hot shard. Monitor via sh.status(), db.collection.getShardDistribution().
30. B - In Queryable Encryption (QE), client-side libmongocrypt encrypts fields and queries. Server stores ciphertext + encrypted index (encrypted
queryable). KMIP/KMS holds keys; server/compromised DBA never sees plaintext, unlike classic at-rest encryption.

# Redis - Complete 30-Question Exam
Beginner Level (Questions 1-10)
**1. What type of data store is Redis primarily classified as?**
A) Disk-based relational database
B) In-memory data structure store
C) Document-oriented NoSQL database
D) Graph database
**2. Which command is used to set a key to hold a string value in Redis?**
A) PUT mykey value
B) SET mykey value
C) ADD mykey value
D) INSERT mykey value
**3. What is the default port on which Redis server runs?**
A) 3306
B) 5432
C) 6379
D) 27017
**4. Which Redis data type is best suited for storing a list of items where order matters and duplicates are allowed?**
A) Set
B) Hash
C) List
D) String
**5. How do you set a key to expire after 60 seconds in Redis?**
A) EXPIRE mykey 60
B) TTL mykey 60
C) TIMEOUT mykey 60
D) DELETE mykey 60
**6. Which command returns all keys matching a pattern? (Note: not recommended for production)**
A) KEYS pattern*
B) SCAN pattern*
C) FIND pattern*
D) SEARCH pattern*
**7. In Redis, what does the command INCR do?**
A) Increments the integer value of a key by one
B) Increments the number of databases
C) Increases memory allocation
D) Creates a new key
**8. Which persistence option in Redis logs every write operation to a file?**
A) RDB
B) AOF (Append Only File)
C) Snapshot
D) Journal
**9. What data type would you use to store an object with multiple fields like user:1001 name, email, age?**
A) List
B) Set
C) Hash
D) Sorted Set
**10. Which command is used to check if a key exists in Redis?**
A) HAS mykey
B) EXISTS mykey
C) CHECK mykey
D) FIND mykey
Answer Key - Beginner Level

--- PAGE 197 ---
1. B) In-memory data structure store - Redis keeps dataset primarily in memory for high performance
2. B) SET mykey value - The fundamental string set command
3. C) 6379 - Standard IANA assigned Redis port (6379 spells MERZ on phone keypad for Alessandro Merz, inventor of Redis name)
4. C) List - Linked list implementation preserving insertion order and allowing duplicates
5. A) EXPIRE mykey 60 - Sets timeout in seconds; alternative SET with EX option also valid
6. A) KEYS pattern* - Returns all matching keys (use SCAN for production to avoid blocking)
7. A) Increments the integer value of a key by one - Atomic increment, creates key with 0 if not exists
8. B) AOF (Append Only File) - Logs every write operation for maximum durability
9. C) Hash - Ideal for objects with field-value pairs
10. B) EXISTS mykey - Returns 1 if exists, 0 otherwise; can check multiple keys in modern Redis
---
Intermediate Level (Questions 11-20)
**11. In Redis 7.0+, what is the difference between SMEMBERS and SSCAN for a large Set?**
A) No difference
B) SMEMBERS blocks server, SSCAN iterates incrementally with cursor
C) SMEMBERS is for Sorted Sets
D) SSCAN only works in cluster mode
**12. Which command atomically sets a key only if it does NOT already exist?**
A) SET mykey value NX
B) SETNX mykey value is deprecated, use SET with NX option; SET mykey value XX
C) SET mykey value NX - correct, NX = Not Exists; XX = only if exists
D) ADD mykey value
**13. As of 2024, Redis changed its license from BSD to which dual license model?**
A) MIT + Apache 2.0
B) SSPLv1 + RSALv2 (Redis Source Available License v2)
C) GPLv3 + Commercial
D) AGPL + MIT
**14. In Redis Cluster, how many hash slots are used for key distribution?**
A) 1024
B) 4096
C) 16384
D) 65536
**15. What is the purpose of Redis Transactions using MULTI/EXEC?**
A) To provide ACID compliance like relational databases
B) To queue commands and execute them atomically without interruption, but without rollback on logical errors
C) To compress multiple commands
D) To replicate across clusters
**16. Which eviction policy in redis.conf will evict least recently used keys among those with expire set when memory limit is reached?**
A) noeviction
B) allkeys-lru
C) volatile-lru
D) allkeys-random
**17. How does Redis Streams (XADD/XREAD) differ from Pub/Sub?**
A) No difference
B) Streams provide persistence, consumer groups, and message replay, Pub/Sub is fire-and-forget
C) Pub/Sub is persistent, Streams are ephemeral
D) Streams only support single consumer
**18. In Redis 7.2+, what does the WAIT command with WAIT 1 5000 accomplish?**
A) Waits for 5000 replicas
B) Blocks until write is acknowledged by at least 1 replica or 5000ms timeout for stronger durability
C) Sleeps server for 5 seconds
D) Waits for client connections
**19. What is the recommended approach for running Lua scripts in Redis 7.0+ for production?**
A) Use EVAL directly with inline scripts
B) Use SCRIPT LOAD and EVALSHA, or Functions with FUNCTION LOAD (persistent, versioned Lua library in Redis 7.0+)
C) Lua is no longer supported

--- PAGE 198 ---
D) Use external interpreter
**20. How do you handle a large sorted set to get top 10 elements with highest scores efficiently?**
A) SMEMBERS and sort client-side
B) ZREVRANGE myzset 0 9 or ZREVRANGE myzset 0 9 WITHSCORES (or ZRANGE with REV in Redis 7.2+ / Redis 8.0)
C) SORT myzset DESC LIMIT 10
D) ZRANGE myzset -10 -1
Answer Key - Intermediate Level
11. B) SMEMBERS blocks server, SSCAN iterates incrementally with cursor - SMEMBERS O(N) blocks; SSCAN is cursor-based non-blocking for
large collections
12. C) SET mykey value NX - NX is Not Exists condition; SETNX is deprecated alias. For context, option A is also correct syntax; best practice is
SET with NX
13. B) SSPLv1 + RSALv2 - Changed March 2024, from BSD-3 to dual SSPL/RSALv2 source-available for Redis 7.4+; Valkey fork created by
Linux Foundation as BSD alternative
14. C) 16384 - Cluster divides keyspace into 16384 slots assigned to nodes, computed via CRC16(key) mod 16384
15. B) To queue commands and execute them atomically without interruption, but without rollback on logical errors - MULTI queues, EXEC
executes atomically isolated; if command fails, others still execute
16. C) volatile-lru - Evicts LRU among keys with expire set; allkeys-lru evicts among all keys regardless of expire
17. B) Streams provide persistence, consumer groups, and message replay, Pub/Sub is fire-and-forget - Streams are durable append-only log
with consumer group acknowledgments (XACK), Pub/Sub messages lost if subscriber offline
18. B) Blocks until write is acknowledged by at least 1 replica or 5000ms timeout - Improves durability guarantee for synchronous replication
needs, returns number of replicas acknowledging
19. B) Use SCRIPT LOAD and EVALSHA, or Functions with FUNCTION LOAD - Redis Functions (Redis 7.0+) provide persistence across
restarts, library management, versioning, better than ephemeral EVAL; EVAL is still valid but less efficient
20. B) ZREVRANGE myzset 0 9 or ZREVRANGE myzset 0 9 WITHSCORES - In Redis 7.2+ unified ZRANGE with REV flag: ZRANGE myzset 0 9
REV. ZREVRANGE remains compatible but ZRANGE is new generic syntax in Redis 8.0 direction
---
Advanced Level (Questions 21-30)
**21. In Redis 8.0 (released May 2025), which major architectural change was introduced regarding Redis Stack modules?**
A) Modules were removed completely
B) 8 core modules (JSON, Search, TimeSeries, Bloom, Cuckoo, CMS, TopK, Vector Sets) integrated into core distribution and query engine
unified with enhanced vector search
C) Only JSON module integrated
D) No change from 7.x
**22. You have a high-throughput write workload and AOF fsync everysec is causing latency spikes. Which Redis 7.0+ feature helps mitigate AOF
rewrite blocking?**
A) Disable AOF
B) Multi-part AOF (MP-AOF) which splits AOF into base and incremental files and decouples rewrites
C) Use RDB only
D) Increase aof-rewrite-percentage
**23. In Redis Cluster with 3 masters and 3 replicas, a client gets MOVED or ASK redirection. What is the difference?**
A) Same meaning
B) MOVED means slot permanently moved to another node, client should update slot map; ASK means slot is in migrating state, client should
send ASKING first and request is one-off to target node
C) ASK means permanent, MOVED means temporary
D) MOVED is for replicas only
**24. How do Redis Vector Sets (introduced in Redis 8.0, previously VSET / Redis Query Engine) store and search embeddings?**
A) As simple string values
B) As new data type with VSIM, VADD, VEMB, VCARD commands supporting HNSW graph for approximate nearest neighbor search with Q8
quantization and built-in FILTER for hybrid search
C) Only via external module
D) They don't support quantization
**25. What is the purpose of the CLIENT TRACKING feature with OPTIN and NOLOOP and its use with RESP3?**
A) For auditing client IPs
B) Server-assisted client-side caching (CSC): server tracks keys and sends invalidation messages; OPTIN caches only when client says CLIENT
CACHING yes, NOLOOP prevents self-invalidation notifications
C) To block clients
D) For replication

--- PAGE 199 ---
**26. You are debugging OOM and see many client output buffer limits exceeded. Which advanced mitigation strategy is best for 2024-2026 Redis
deployments?**
A) Increase client-output-buffer-limit to unlimited
B) Set client-output-buffer-limit pubsub 32mb 8mb 60, monitor SLOWLOG, consider unifying to use Streams with consumer groups instead of
Pub/Sub for slow consumers, use client-output-buffer-limit-tracking and implement streaming response backpressure
C) Restart server
D) Delete keys
**27. Explain the atomicity and performance trade-off of Lua scripts vs. Functions vs. MULTI/EXEC in Redis 7.2/8.0?**
A) All identical
B) Lua/Eval and Functions run atomically blocking all other commands during execution (script is atomic) and should be kept short (<5ms);
MULTI/EXEC queues but EXEC still atomic without script's ability to do server-side logic; Functions persist on disk and replicate vs scripts
ephemeral; Functions offer improved isolation with function flags like no-writes
C) MULTI/EXEC is scripted
D) Functions are not atomic
**28. In a Redis Sentinel setup (3 sentinels, 1 master, 2 replicas), what quorum and failover flow occurs when master goes down in Redis 7.2+?**
A) Single sentinel decides
B) Sentinels achieve quorum (e.g., quorum 2), elect leader via Raft-like election, leader promotes best replica (based on replica priority, replication
offset, runid), reconfigures other replicas to new master, and notifies clients via +switch-master pubsub channel; clients using Sentinel-aware
clients auto-discover new master
C) Manual failover only
D) Cluster replaces Sentinel
**29. How does Redis handle big keys (e.g., hash with 10M fields) causing latency in Redis 7.4+/8.0, and what tool identifies them?**
A) No impact
B) Big keys cause blocking on DEL, EXPIRE, etc. Use --bigkeys or MEMORY USAGE, redis-cli --bigkeys sampling, SCAN + HLEN/LLEN, and
use UNLINK (async delete) instead of DEL, HSCAN for incremental access, and active defrag; Redis 8.0 improves async free efficiency and adds
MDEFRAG
C) Redis auto-shards big keys
D) Use KEYS *
**30. For a financial ledger requiring strong consistency and zero data loss on single node failure, which 2024-2026 Redis configuration is
closest?**
A) Default async replication
B) WAIT 1 + AOF always fsync + replication + enable appendfsync always + min-replicas-to-write 1 + Redis 8.0 durability improvement: Using 1
primary + 2 replicas across AZs, AOF on each, and WAIT command in application critical path; or evaluate Redis Raft / Valkey alternative for
strong consensus (but official Redis remains eventual consistency). For zero RPO, use disk-backed strong consistency system alongside Redis
as cache
C) Disable persistence
D) Use RDB only every 5 minutes
Answer Key - Advanced Level
21. B) 8 core modules integrated into core distribution and query engine unified - Redis 8.0 Community Edition bundles 8 previously Stack
modules (now core capabilities) and unifies FT.SEARCH, JSON, Vector Sets under one binary for easier deployment
22. B) Multi-part AOF (MP-AOF) - Introduced Redis 7.0, splits AOF into multiple files: base files + incr files to avoid blocking rewrite and improve
reload performance; controlled by aof-rewrite logic and manifest file tracking
23. B) MOVED means slot permanently moved to another node, client should update slot map; ASK means slot is in migrating state - Core cluster
redirection logic: MOVED is persistent configuration change, ASK is transient during migration requiring ASKING command
24. B) As new data type with VSIM, VADD, VEMB, VCARD commands supporting HNSW - Vector Sets GA in Redis 8.0 use HNSW, support
Q8/NOQUANT/BINARY quantization, attributes for filtering, and hybrid search combining vector similarity + scalar filters in single command
25. B) Server-assisted client-side caching (CSC) - RESP3 invalidation messages allow clients like Java, Node to cache locally; TRACKING ON,
OPTIN mode requires explicit CLIENT CACHING yes per command; NOLOOP avoids notifications caused by same connection
26. B) Set client-output-buffer-limit pubsub 32mb 8mb 60, monitor SLOWLOG, consider Streams - Slow consumers kill memory; best practice
limits buffer, disconnects abusive slow consumers, replaces broadcast Pub/Sub with consumer groups for durability and pull-based flow control
27. B) Lua/Eval and Functions run atomically blocking all other commands - All scripts atomic, single-threaded execution blocks server; keep
scripts <5ms, use SCRIPT KILL, function flags (NO-WRITES, ALLOW-OOM) introduced in 7.0+ to allow engine optimization and better isolation;
MULTI/EXEC simply atomic queue without logic
28. B) Sentinels achieve quorum, elect leader via Raft-like election, leader promotes best replica - Classic Sentinel failover requires majority
agreement, leader election reduces split-brain, replica selection uses priority, replica offset lag, and run ID
29. B) Big keys cause blocking on DEL, EXPIRE, etc. Use --bigkeys or MEMORY USAGE - Big keys block event loop on delete because free is
synchronous for small allocs but UNLINK offloads to lazyfree thread (bio thread); identify via redis-cli --bigkeys, MEMORY STATS, LATENCY
DOCTOR, and slowlog; mitigate via chunking and hashes with hashtable expansion controlled by active-expire effort
30. B) WAIT 1 + AOF always fsync + replication - Official Redis is AP oriented; strongest durability is always fsync (performance cost) + WAIT for
replica ack + min-replicas-to-write to prevent writes without replica; true zero data loss strong consistency needs consensus module like RedisRaft

--- PAGE 200 ---
or external ACID DB; architecture in 2025-2026 increasingly uses Redis as cache tier with relational DB as source of truth for financial ledger,
Redis 8.0 improved durability but not CP

# Apache Kafka - Certification Exam (39)
**Course:** Apache Kafka Fundamentals to Advanced Architecture
**Version:** Accurate for Kafka 3.6 - 4.0 (2024-2026) - KRaft Mode, KIP Updates
**Total Questions:** 30 | **Passing Marks:** 70%
**Duration:** 90 Minutes
---
Section A: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
Q1. What is Apache Kafka primarily designed for?
A) Relational database storage
B) Distributed event streaming platform for high-throughput publish-subscribe messaging
C) Web server for HTTP requests
D) Batch processing framework only
Q2. In Kafka, what is a Topic?
A) A physical server running Kafka
B) A category or feed name to which records are published
C) A consumer that reads data
D) A configuration file
Q3. What is a Kafka Broker?
A) A client that produces data
B) A Kafka server that stores data and serves clients, part of a cluster
C) A partition within a topic
D) A Zookeeper node
Q4. As of Kafka 4.0 (2024-2025), what is the status of ZooKeeper?
A) Still required for all clusters
B) Removed completely; KRaft (Kafka Raft metadata mode) is the only mode, ZooKeeper support dropped
C) Optional but recommended
D) Replaced by etcd
Q5. What does a Kafka Producer do?
A) Reads messages from topics
B) Publishes (writes) records/events to Kafka topics
C) Deletes topics
D) Manages consumer offsets automatically
Q6. What is a Kafka Partition?
A) A backup of the broker
B) An ordered, immutable sequence of records; a topic is split into partitions for parallelism
C) A consumer group
D) A type of compression
Q7. What is the default retention behavior in Kafka?
A) Messages are deleted immediately after consumption
B) Messages are retained for a configurable time/size (default 7 days / 168 hours) regardless of consumption
C) Messages are retained forever by default
D) Messages are retained for 1 hour only
Q8. Which protocol do Kafka clients use to communicate with brokers?
A) HTTP/REST by default
B) Custom binary TCP protocol over port 9092
C) FTP

--- PAGE 202 ---
D) AMQP only
Q9. What is a Consumer Group in Kafka?
A) A set of producers working together
B) A set of consumers that cooperate to consume a topic, with each partition consumed by only one consumer in the group
C) A group of brokers
D) A replication factor setting
Q10. What is the role of a Kafka Record Key?
A) Only for encryption
B) Determines the partition for the record (via hashing) and can be used for compaction and ordering guarantees
C) Is mandatory for every record
D) Stores offset information
Answer Key - Beginner Level
| Question | Answer | Explanation |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Q1 | B | Kafka is a distributed event streaming platform, not a database or web server. |
| Q2 | B | Topic is a logical feed name. Records are appended to topics. |
| Q3 | B | Broker is the Kafka server instance. |
| Q4 | B | KIP-833: Kafka 4.0 (released 2025) removed ZooKeeper support. KRaft is now the sole metadata quorum. |
| Q5 | B | Producer publishes/writes events. |
| Q6 | B | Partitions provide ordering within partition and allow parallelism. |
| Q7 | B | Kafka retains by time/size, not by consumption. Default log.retention.hours=168. |
| Q8 | B | Native binary protocol; 9092 is default plaintext port. |
| Q9 | B | Consumer group enables load balancing and fault tolerance. |
| Q10 | B | Key determines partition via DefaultPartitioner hash(key) % partitions; null key uses round-robin/sticky. Optional field. |

Section B: Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
Q11. In KRaft mode, what component replaces ZooKeeper for metadata management?
A) A separate etcd cluster
B) KRaft Quorum Controllers using Raft consensus with an internal __cluster_metadata topic
C) Kafka Connect
D) A single active controller broker with no quorum
Q12. What does acks=all (or acks=-1) mean for a producer?
A) No acknowledgment needed, fastest
B) Leader only acknowledgment
C) Leader waits for full set of in-sync replicas (ISR) to acknowledge before responding
D) Wait for all brokers in cluster regardless of ISR
Q13. What is ISR (In-Sync Replica) in Kafka?
A) All replicas configured
B) The subset of replicas that are currently caught up with the leader within replica.lag.time.max.ms / replica.lag.time.max deprecated, now based
on fetch progress
C) Only the leader replica
D) Replicas in another datacenter
Q14. How does Kafka achieve exactly-once semantics (EOS) end-to-end?
A) Impossible in Kafka
B) Using Idempotent producer (enable.idempotence=true) + Transactions API (transactional.id) + isolation.level=read_committed on consumer
C) Just set acks=all
D) Use consumer auto-commit
Q15. What is log compaction in Kafka?

--- PAGE 203 ---
A) Compressing logs with gzip
B) Kafka retains at least the last known value for each record key within a topic, removing older values for same key; used for changelog topics
C) Deleting all logs
D) Merging partitions
Q16. In Kafka 3.6+, what is the new Group Coordinator protocol for consumers (KIP-848)?
A) Classic consumer group protocol remains only option
B) Next-gen consumer group protocol (Consumer protocol) with improved rebalancing, client-side assignment, and support for new group types,
previewed as KIP-848
C) Removed consumer groups entirely
D) ZooKeeper-based coordination
Q17. What is the purpose of min.insync.replicas?
A) Minimum number of brokers in cluster
B) Minimum number of replicas in ISR that must acknowledge a write when acks=all - otherwise produce fails with NotEnoughReplicas
C) Minimum consumer count
D) Minimum partition count
Q18. Which of the following is TRUE about Kafka partitions ordering?
A) Total order across all partitions of a topic
B) Strict order guaranteed only within a single partition; no order across partitions
C) No ordering guarantees at all
D) Order by timestamp across partitions
Q19. What does Kafka Tiered Storage (KIP-405, early access in 3.6+, GA improvements in
4.0) do?
A) Deletes old data faster
B) Separates compute and storage by offloading completed log segments to remote storage (S3/GCS/HDFS) while brokers retain hot data
C) Replaces partitions
D) Is a client-side cache
Q20. How are consumer offsets managed in modern Kafka (post 0.9)?
A) Stored in ZooKeeper only
B) Stored in an internal topic __consumer_offsets (50 partitions by default) managed by Group Coordinator
C) Stored locally on consumer
D) Not stored
Answer Key - Intermediate Level
| Question | Answer | Explanation |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Q11 | B | KRaft uses Raft quorum of controllers; metadata log __cluster_metadata replaces ZooKeeper. |
| Q12 | C | acks=all waits for all ISR to ack, strongest durability. Requires min.insync.replicas. |
| Q13 | B | ISR = replicas fully in-sync with leader. Out-of-sync removed from ISR. |
| Q14 | B | EOS = Idempotence (PID + sequence numbers to avoid duplicates) + Transactions (atomic writes across partitions) + read_committed
to filter aborted txns. Requires Kafka Streams or transactional consume-process-produce. |
| Q15 | B | Compaction keeps latest value per key; configured via cleanup.policy=compact. |
| Q16 | B | KIP-848 introduces KConsumer next-gen protocol, simplifies rebalance with KGroup, adopted in clients in Kafka 4.0. |
| Q17 | B | Ensures durability threshold; if ISR < min.insync.replicas and acks=all, NotEnoughReplicasException. |
| Q18 | B | Kafka only guarantees order inside partition. For total order, use 1 partition (sacrifices throughput). |
| Q19 | B | Tiered storage enables infinite retention cost-effectively; brokers act as hot cache. |
| Q20 | B | Since 0.9, offsets are in __consumer_offsets, not ZK. __consumer_offsets is compacted. |

Q29. You have a topic with 12 partitions and 3 consumers in same group with
CooperativeSticky assignor (KIP-429). One consumer dies. What happens?

B) Incremental cooperative rebalance: only partitions of dead consumer are revoked and reassigned, other consumers continue consuming

Answer Key - Advanced Level
| Question | Answer | Explanation |
|----------|--------|-------------|
| Q21 | B | KRaft combined mode uses broker,controller; controller.quorum.voters e.g. 1@host1:9093,2@host2:9093. In dedicated mode nodes
are controller only or broker only. |
| Q22 | B | RF=3, min.isr=2 tolerates 1 failure. If 2 failures, ISR=1 < min.isr => writes fail for acks=all. |
| Q23 | B | Idempotent producer uses PID + monotonically increasing sequence per partition. Broker checks seq to dedup. After 5 in-flight limit
breached, DuplicateSequenceException logic. |
| Q24 | B | Unclean election can cause data loss (committed data on old leader not in new). Default false preserves consistency over availability. |
| Q25 | B | Kafka Streams state stores use changelog topics for recovery. KIP-892+ Tiered state, IQv2. |
| Q26 | B | StickyPartitioner avoids round-robin small batches; batching + linger + zstd/lz4 maximizes throughput; acks=1 may increase throughput
but acks=all is recommended for durability, tuned with separate settings. |
| Q27 | B | KRaft metadata log with snapshots; brokers replicate metadata log. KIP-853 in Kafka 3.7/3.8 improves snapshot generation without
blocking. |
| Q28 | B | read_committed hides aborted txns; LSO advances only when txns commit/abort; prevents reading uncommitted transactional data. |
| Q29 | B | CooperativeSticky (incremental rebalance protocol) introduced by KIP-429 avoids eager stop-the-world; only affected partitions move,
minimizing churn. |
| Q30 | B | Kafka 4.0 is major: ZK removal, Scala client removal, message format v0/v1 removal, listener validation stricter, KRaft mandatory
controller listener. Security hardening per KIPs. |
