40_Microservices.md
# Microservices Architecture - Comprehensive Examination
**Course Code:** 40_Microservices
**Total Questions:** 30 (10 Beginner, 10 Intermediate, 10 Advanced)
**Format:** Multiple Choice - 4 Options per Question
**Syllabus Coverage:** 2024-2026 Standards, Cloud-Native Patterns, Service Mesh, Observability, DDD, Event-Driven Architecture
---
Part A: Beginner Level (10 Questions)
Q1. What is the core principle of Microservices Architecture?
A) Building a single monolithic application with modular code
B) Structuring an application as a collection of small, loosely coupled, independently deployable services
C) Using only micro-controllers for deployment
D) Requiring all services to share a single centralized database
Q2. In microservices, what is the primary purpose of an API Gateway?
A) To store all microservices source code
B) To provide a single entry point for clients, handling routing, authentication, rate limiting, and aggregation
C) To replace all databases with in-memory cache
D) To compile microservices into a monolith
Q3. Which communication style is considered synchronous in microservices?
A) Message Queue with RabbitMQ
B) Event Streaming with Apache Kafka
C) REST over HTTP / gRPC synchronous request-response
D) Event Sourcing with EventStoreDB
Q4. What does the term "Bounded Context" from Domain-Driven Design (DDD) mean for
microservices?
A) The maximum memory a service can use
B) A clear boundary within a domain where a particular model and language applies, often mapping to a microservice
C) The network timeout limit for service calls
D) The physical data center location
Q5. What is containerization's role in microservices as of 2024-2026?
A) It is deprecated in favor of VMs
B) It packages a service and its dependencies into a portable, isolated unit (e.g., Docker, OCI containers) crucial for deployment on Kubernetes
C) It only applies to frontend applications
D) It prevents services from communicating
Q6. Why is database-per-service pattern recommended?
A) To increase tight coupling between services
B) To ensure loose coupling, independent scalability, and technology heterogeneity; each service owns its data
C) To reduce storage costs by 90%
D) To make distributed transactions easier
Q7. What is service discovery in microservices?
A) A manual documentation process
B) Automatic detection of service instances and their network locations (e.g., via Kubernetes DNS, Consul, Eureka)
C) Finding bugs in production
D) Discovering new programming languages
Q8. Which of the following is a key characteristic of a well-designed microservice?
A) Highly dependent on other services to start
B) Single Responsibility, independently deployable, owned by a small team, and focused on one business capability

--- PAGE 207 ---
C) Must be written in the same language as all other services
D) Cannot have its own CI/CD pipeline
Q9. What is the difference between orchestration and choreography?
A) Orchestration uses a central controller (e.g., workflow engine), while choreography uses events where services react independently without
central control
B) They are identical concepts
C) Orchestration is only for monoliths
D) Choreography requires Kubernetes
Q10. As of 2024-2026, what is the most common platform for orchestrating microservices
at scale?
A) Bare metal without containerization
B) Kubernetes (K8s) with GitOps tools like ArgoCD / Flux
C) Single Docker host with docker-compose only
D) FTP servers
Answer Key - Beginner Level
1. B - Microservices are loosely coupled, independently deployable services around business domains.
2. B - API Gateway is the edge proxy for cross-cutting concerns.
3. C - REST/gRPC request-response is synchronous.
4. B - Bounded Context defines domain boundaries.
5. B - Containers provide portability and isolation.
6. B - Database-per-service ensures autonomy and decoupling.
7. B - Service discovery automates location of instances.
8. B - Single responsibility and independence are fundamental.
9. A - Orchestration vs Choreography is central vs decentralized coordination.
10. B - Kubernetes is de facto standard in 2024-2026.
---
Part B: Intermediate Level (10 Questions)
Q11. In the Saga pattern, how are distributed transactions managed across
microservices?
A) Using traditional 2PC (Two-Phase Commit) with XA transactions across all services
B) As a sequence of local transactions, each publishing an event triggering the next, with compensating transactions for rollback on failure
C) By sharing a single database transaction
D) Sagas are not used for transactions
Q12. What is the primary benefit of a Service Mesh (e.g., Istio 1.22+, Linkerd) in 2025?
A) To write business logic faster
B) To abstract and manage service-to-service communication, providing mTLS, traffic management, retries, circuit breaking, and observability via
sidecars / ambient mode
C) To replace API Gateway completely
D) To store persistent data
Q13. You have cascading failures when Service A calls Service B synchronously. Which
pattern prevents this?
A) Singleton Pattern
B) Circuit Breaker Pattern (e.g., Resilience4j, Istio circuit breaker) + Retry with exponential backoff and bulkhead isolation
C) Database-per-service pattern
D) MVC pattern
Q14. What is CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation) in microservices
context?
A) Using same model for read and write for simplicity

--- PAGE 208 ---
B) Separating the data models for write (commands) and read (queries), often combined with Event Sourcing, to optimize scalability and
performance
C) A database replication strategy only
D) A testing framework
Q15. As of 2024-2026, what is the industry standard for observability (tracing, metrics,
logs) in microservices?
A) Proprietary agents only
B) OpenTelemetry (OTel) for instrumentation, with Prometheus for metrics, Jaeger/Tempo for tracing, and Loki/ELK for logs, visualized in Grafana
C) Console.log statements
D) Only health check endpoints
Q16. How does Event-Driven Architecture with Kafka differ from REST for inter-service
communication in microservices?
A) Kafka is synchronous and tightly coupled, REST is asynchronous
B) Kafka provides asynchronous, durable, decoupled communication with replay and high throughput; REST is synchronous request-response
suitable for immediate queries
C) Kafka cannot be used in microservices
D) REST is always more scalable than Kafka
Q17. What is the Strangler Fig Pattern?
A) A pattern to kill production servers
B) Incrementally migrating a monolith to microservices by gradually replacing specific functionalities with new services behind a facade/proxy
C) A database sharding technique
D) A security attack pattern
Q18. In Kubernetes, how do you achieve zero-downtime deployment for a microservice in
2025?
A) Delete all pods at once and recreate
B) Use RollingUpdate deployment strategy with readiness/liveness probes, PodDisruptionBudgets, and possibly Blue-Green or Canary via Argo
Rollouts / Flagger
C) Stop traffic for 10 minutes
D) Deploy only on weekends
Q19. What problem does the Backend for Frontend (BFF) pattern solve?
A) It eliminates the need for frontends
B) It creates tailored API gateways per client type (web, mobile, IoT) to provide optimized aggregation and avoid one-size-fits-all gateway
C) It merges all backends into one monolith
D) It is a database design pattern
Q20. Which consistency model is typically adopted in microservices with
database-per-service?
A) Strong immediate consistency across all microservices via distributed locks
B) Eventual Consistency, accepting temporary inconsistency and using events/sagas to converge, aligning with CAP theorem and BASE
C) No consistency at all
D) ACID across all microservices via 2PC
Answer Key - Intermediate Level
11. B - Saga uses local transactions + events + compensations, not 2PC.
12. B - Service mesh handles L4-L7 communication, mTLS, resiliency, observability.
13. B - Circuit Breaker stops cascading failures; bulkhead isolates resources.
14. B - CQRS separates write and read models.
15. B - OpenTelemetry is CNCF standard in 2024-2026 for unified observability.
16. B - Event-driven with Kafka is async/decoupled/durable; REST is sync.
17. B - Strangler Fig enables incremental monolith decomposition.
18. B - RollingUpdate + probes + advanced deployment controllers for zero-downtime.

--- PAGE 209 ---
19. B - BFF provides client-specific API experiences.
20. B - Eventual consistency is pragmatic choice for distributed microservices.
---
Part C: Advanced Level (10 Questions)
Q21. In Istio Ambient Mesh (GA in 2024) vs Sidecar mode, what is the architectural shift?
A) No shift, both are identical
B) Ambient removes per-pod sidecar proxies, using L4 ztunnel node-level proxy and optional L7 waypoint proxies per service account,
significantly reducing resource overhead, latency, and complexity
C) Ambient adds more sidecars per pod
D) Ambient only works with VMs
Q22. You are designing a high-throughput order service processing 100k events/sec. You
use Event Sourcing with Kafka and need exactly-once processing semantics. What
approach is valid in Kafka 3.6+ / 2024-2026?
A) At-most-once is the only option
B) Use idempotent producers (enable.idempotence=true) + transactional API with transaction.id + consumer with isolation.level=read_committed
and idempotent consumer logic / outbox pattern + Kafka Streams exactly-once v2 (EOS v2)
C) Use REST instead of Kafka
D) Disable all acknowledgments
Q23. Considering the CAP theorem and microservices deployed across multiple regions
(multi-region active-active in 2025), how do you design for partition tolerance?
A) Sacrifice partition tolerance and use single region
B) Design for AP (Availability + Partition Tolerance) with eventual consistency, CRDTs / conflict-free replicated data types, and use patterns like
global Kafka with MirrorMaker 2 / Confluent Cluster Linking, or sacrifice strong consistency for availability using Dynamo-style quorum
C) Use 2PC across regions for perfect consistency and availability
D) CAP theorem does not apply to microservices
Q24. Advanced Observability: You have high cardinality metrics explosion (user-id label)
causing Prometheus OOM. As per 2024-2026 best practices, what is correct remediation?
A) Keep high cardinality labels, scale Prometheus infinitely
B) Remove high-cardinality labels, use tracing with exemplars linking metrics to traces (OpenTelemetry exemplars), aggregate at edge, use
recording rules, and push high-cardinality data to Tempo/Jaeger or logs to Loki instead of metrics
C) Delete all metrics
D) Switch to Nagios
Q25. What is eBPF's role in next-gen microservices networking and security (Cilium,
2024-2026)?
A) No role
B) eBPF allows kernel-level high-performance networking, load balancing without kube-proxy, L3-L7 enforcement, transparent mTLS, and deep
observability without sidecars (Cilium Service Mesh, Tetragon for runtime security)
C) Only for UI rendering
D) Replaces Docker images
Q26. When applying Domain Decomposition strategies (Decompose by Subdomain vs
Decompose by Business Capability), which advanced consideration differentiates them in
large enterprise DDD?
A) They are the same
B) Decompose by Subdomain aligns with DDD subdomains (core/supporting/generic), better for strategic modeling and avoiding anemic services;
Business Capability aligns with value streams and organization structure. In practice, hybrid approach with Team Topologies (Stream-aligned
teams) is recommended for 2024-2026 orgs
C) Only business capability is valid
D) Subdomain decomposition requires shared database

--- PAGE 210 ---
Q27. You implement API versioning in microservices for breaking changes in 2025. What
is the most resilient, production-grade approach?
A) Never version APIs
B) URI versioning (e.g., /v1/orders) combined with backward compatible additive changes, schema evolution with Protobuf/Avro compatibility
rules, consumer-driven contract testing (Pact), and parallel deploy of v1 and v2 with BFF translation and gradual sunset via OpenAPI +
deprecation headers
C) Only use URL query param ?version=1 without testing
D) Change database directly
Q28. Chaos Engineering in microservices (LitmusChaos, Chaos Mesh 2024+) - Which
experiment validates Saga compensation logic?
A) CPU hog experiment only
B) Inject pod failure / network latency / Kafka broker down / DB failure during middle of Saga (e.g., after Payment but before Inventory), and verify
compensating transactions (refund payment) executed and event log reaches consistent terminal state, with observability proving no stuck
transaction
C) Delete GitHub repo
D) Chaos engineering is not applicable to Sagas
Q29. Security 2025: You have zero-trust microservices. Which layers constitute
defense-in-depth for inter-service communication?
A) Only firewall
B) 1) mTLS with SPIFFE/SPIRE identities via Istio/Cilium 2) L7 AuthorizationPolicy / OPA/Kylo 3) JWT validation at gateway + fine-grained token
exchange 4) NetworkPolicies + CiliumNetworkPolicy 5) Secrets management via Vault / External Secrets Operator with rotation 6) Supply chain
security (Sigstore cosign, SBOM)
C) Username/password in config file
D) Disable security for performance
Q30. Performance anti-pattern: You have Chatty services (N+1 calls) - Service A calls
Service B 100 times per request leading to p99 latency 2s. What 2024-2026 solution is
recommended?
A) Increase timeouts
B) Apply GraphQL federation or BFF aggregation layer, implement gRPC streaming / batch API (e.g., GetOrdersBatch), use DataLoader pattern
for batching/caching, place cache via Redis/Valkey with read-through, and consider Materialized View via CQRS to denormalize data and
eliminate N+1
C) Add more pods without fixing
D) Use synchronous calls in loop
Answer Key - Advanced Level
21. B - Ambient Mesh is sideless, reducing cost and ops overhead vs sidecars.
22. B - Idempotent + transactional producer/consumer + EOS v2 achieves effective exactly-once.
23. B - Multi-region requires AP + eventual consistency, CRDTs, async replication.
24. B - High cardinality belongs in traces/logs with exemplars, not Prometheus labels.
25. B - eBPF powers Cilium for performant, secure, observable networking without kube-proxy/sidecars.
26. B - Subdomain vs Capability requires strategic DDD + Team Topologies; hybrid is practical.
27. B - Robust versioning uses URI versions, compatibility, contract testing, parallel deploys.
28. B - Chaos experiments should crash mid-Saga to verify compensation.
29. B - Zero-trust requires mTLS + L7 authz + JWT + NetPol + secrets + supply chain.
30. B - Fix N+1 with batch APIs, GraphQL federation, DataLoader, caching, CQRS views.

# CI/CD Pipelines - Certification Exam (ID: 41)
**Total Questions: 30 | Duration: 90 Minutes | Passing Score: 70%**
*Accurate for 2024-2026 tooling: GitHub Actions, GitLab CI, Jenkins, Docker, Kubernetes, IaC, Security*
---
Section A: Beginner (10 Questions)
Q1. What is the primary purpose of a CI/CD pipeline?
A) To manage project documentation
B) To automate building, testing, and deploying code changes
C) To monitor server hardware only
D) To design user interfaces
Q2. What does CI stand for in CI/CD?
A) Continuous Integration
B) Code Inspection
C) Centralized Infrastructure
D) Container Initialization
Q3. In GitHub Actions, what file defines a workflow?
A) A .py file in the root directory
B) A YAML file in .github/workflows/
C) A JSON file in .github/actions/
D) A Dockerfile
Q4. Which command is typically used to build a Docker image in a CI pipeline?
A) docker push
B) docker run
C) docker build -t myapp .
D) docker pull
Q5. What is the purpose of git clone in a CI job?
A) To deploy to production
B) To download the repository source code into the runner
C) To delete old branches
D) To create a Docker image
Q6. What is a "runner" in the context of GitHub Actions or GitLab CI?
A) A type of automated test
B) An agent/server that executes the pipeline jobs
C) A deployment approval tool
D) A code linter
Q7. Which practice ensures that code in the main branch is always in a deployable state?
A) Pushing directly to main without tests
B) Continuous Integration with automated tests on every pull request
C) Manual testing once a month
D) Disabling all pipelines
Q8. What does a Jenkinsfile contain?
A) Database credentials only
B) The pipeline-as-code definition using Groovy DSL
C) HTML for Jenkins dashboard
D) User authentication logs

--- PAGE 212 ---
Q9. In CI/CD terminology, what is an artifact?
A) A bug report
B) A file or package produced by a build, such as a JAR, Docker image, or binary
C) A chat message from the pipeline
D) A failed job log only
Q10. What is the difference between Continuous Delivery and Continuous Deployment?
A) There is no difference
B) Continuous Delivery requires manual approval to deploy to production, while Continuous Deployment automatically deploys to production after
passing checks
C) Continuous Deployment is only for mobile apps
D) Continuous Delivery is slower than Continuous Integration
Answer Key - Beginner
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q1 | B | CI/CD automates the software delivery lifecycle: build, test, and deploy. |
| Q2 | A | CI = Continuous Integration - merging and testing code frequently. |
| Q3 | B | GitHub Actions workflows are YAML files stored in .github/workflows/ |
| Q4 | C | docker build -t myapp . builds image from Dockerfile in current context. |
| Q5 | B | Runners clone the repo to get source code to build/test. |
| Q6 | B | Runners are the compute agents that execute jobs. |
| Q7 | B | CI with automated PR checks prevents broken code from entering main. |
| Q8 | B | Jenkinsfile is pipeline-as-code written in Groovy-based DSL. |
| Q9 | B | Artifacts are build outputs preserved for testing or deployment. |
| Q10 | B | Delivery = manual prod release gate; Deployment = fully automated to prod. |

Section B: Intermediate (10 Questions)
Q11. In GitHub Actions, how do you prevent concurrent deployments to production that
could cause race conditions?
A) Using concurrency: group: production, cancel-in-progress: false
B) Using if: always()
C) Deleting the workflow file
D) Using runs-on: ubuntu-latest only
Q12. What is the purpose of actions/cache@v4 in a GitHub Actions workflow for Node.js

A) To deploy to AWS
B) To cache dependencies like node_modules or ~/.m2 to speed up subsequent runs
C) To delete logs
D) To create a Docker network
Q13. In GitLab CI, what keyword defines the stages execution order, and what file holds

A) stages: in .gitlab-ci.yml, executed top-down
B) phases: in gitlab.json
C) steps: in Dockerfile
D) jobs: in .github/workflows/main.yml
Q14. Which strategy is best for zero-downtime deployments in Kubernetes via a CI/CD

A) Delete all pods then deploy new ones
B) Use RollingUpdate strategy or Blue/Green deployment with readiness probes

--- PAGE 213 ---
D) Use kubectl delete deployment every time
Q15. What does needs: [build, test] signify in GitHub Actions or GitLab DAG pipelines?
A) It skips those jobs
B) It defines job dependencies, creating a directed acyclic graph (DAG) to run jobs after prerequisites complete
C) It deletes build and test artifacts
D) It only works on Windows
Q16. You need to securely use secrets (e.g., AWS credentials, NPM tokens) in a CI
pipeline. Which is the correct approach in 2024-2026?
A) Hardcode them in the YAML file and commit
B) Store them as encrypted secrets in GitHub Actions Secrets / GitLab CI/CD Variables / Jenkins Credentials with OIDC federation to cloud (avoid
long-lived keys)
C) Email them to the team
D) Put them in Dockerfile ENV
Q17. What is pipeline artifact caching vs. Docker layer caching?
A) They are identical
B) Pipeline artifact caching saves dependencies/files between workflow runs; Docker layer caching reuses unchanged Dockerfile layers to speed
up docker build
C) Docker layer caching is for JavaScript only
D) Pipeline caching deletes images
Q18. In Jenkins declarative pipeline, what does post { failure { ... } } do?
A) It runs the block only if the pipeline or stage fails, ideal for notifications/Slack alerts
B) It always deletes the Jenkins server
C) It compiles the code twice
D) It skips all tests
Q19. How do you implement Trunk-Based Development with feature flags in CI/CD?
A) Long-lived feature branches merged quarterly
B) Short-lived branches or direct commits to trunk, with feature flags (e.g., LaunchDarkly, OpenFeature) to hide incomplete features in production,
enabling true CI
C) Disable CI entirely
D) Use only manual FTP uploads
Q20. What is the significance of reusable workflows (workflow_call) in GitHub Actions
since 2022?
A) They are deprecated
B) They allow you to create centrally maintained workflows that can be called from other repos, enabling DRY and governance at scale (platform
team pattern)
C) They only run locally
D) They prevent Docker builds
Answer Key - Intermediate
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q11 | A | concurrency group ensures only one prod deployment at a time, queuing others. Critical for 2024+ GitHub Actions. |
| Q12 | B | actions/cache@v4 caches dependency directories, reducing build time by 50-80%. |
| Q13 | A | GitLab uses .gitlab-ci.yml with stages: defining order: build -> test -> deploy. |
| Q14 | B | RollingUpdate with readinessProbe/livenessProbe ensures zero-downtime; Blue/Green is also valid via Argo Rollouts. |
| Q15 | B | needs creates DAG dependencies, enabling parallelization and fail-fast. |
| Q16 | B | Never hardcode secrets; use platform secret stores + OIDC federation (e.g., GitHub OIDC to AWS) for short-lived credentials. |
| Q17 | B | Two distinct cache types both optimize builds but at different layers. |
| Q18 | A | post { failure } handles failure notifications, cleanup, etc. |
| Q19 | B | Trunk-based + feature flags = core modern CI/CD practice - small batches, flags control rollout. |
| Q20 | B | Reusable workflows (on: workflow_call) enable enterprise-scale standardization. |

Section C: Advanced (10 Questions)
Q21. How would you design a secure, scalable GitHub Actions workflow for building and
pushing multi-arch Docker images with provenance attestation in 2025?
A) Use docker/build-push-action@v6 with platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64, enable cache-from/to: type=gha, and add
actions/attest-build-provenance@v2 with SLSA provenance + OIDC; scan with Trivy before push
B) Use docker build locally and email the image
C) Push without scanning and use root user in container
D) Disable GitHub Actions and use FTP
Q22. Your org uses ArgoCD for GitOps. What is the correct CI/CD separation of concerns?
A) CI builds and pushes image, then updates image tag in GitOps repo (via PR or Image Updater); CD (ArgoCD) detects drift and syncs cluster to
desired state declaratively
B) CI directly runs kubectl apply to production from CI runner
C) ArgoCD builds Docker images
D) CI and CD must be the same Jenkins job
Q23. In high-performance monorepo CI (e.g., Bazel/Nx/Turbo), what 2024-2026 technique
prevents running all tests on every PR?
A) Run all tests always
B) Use Affected analysis / dependency graph + remote cache (e.g., nx affected --base=origin/main, Bazel remote cache & remote execution) to
run only impacted targets + distributed caching
C) Disable CI for monorepos
D) Use git push --force always
Q24. You need to implement pipeline compliance for SLSA Level 3 and SBOM generation.
What tooling fits?
A) No tools needed
B) Use slsa-framework/slsa-github-generator, anchore/sbom-action or syft to generate SPDX/CycloneDX SBOM, cosign to sign images, trivy +
grype for vuln scanning, and enforce via policy controllers (OPA/Kyverno)
C) Manually write SBOM in Notepad
D) Use MD5 only
Q25. How do you prevent deployment of vulnerable log4j or compromised dependency in
pipeline, covering 2024 supply chain attacks?
A) Rely on developer memory
B) Implement pipeline gates: Dependabot/Renovate for updates, npm audit/pip-audit, Snyk or Trivy FS scan, Sigstore verification, lockfiles
enforcement, and blocking deployments if CVSS > threshold or malicious package detected (e.g., via Socket.dev)
C) Ignore CVEs
D) Only deploy on Fridays
Q26. Advanced: You have a Jenkins controller with 500 jobs migrating to GitHub Actions
at enterprise scale. What is the modern 2024-2026 migration strategy?
A) Rewrite everything manually in one night
B) Use GitHub Actions Importer (gh actions import), convert Jenkinsfile to YAML via actions/github-actions-importer, implement self-hosted ARC
(Actions Runner Controller) on Kubernetes for elasticity, use composite actions + reusable workflows for reusability, and OIDC federation
C) Keep Jenkins forever without changes
D) Delete all pipelines
Q27. What is progressive delivery in CD, and how would you implement it with a pipeline?
A) Deploy everything to 100% at once
B) Gradually roll out changes using Canary / Blue-Green / Feature Flags / Ring deployments orchestrated by Flagger or Argo Rollouts in pipeline;
analyze metrics (Prometheus/Datadog) and auto-rollback on SLO breach via automated analysis
C) Only deploy documentation
D) Manual SCP copy

--- PAGE 215 ---
Q28. You need ephemeral preview environments for each PR for a microservices app with
10 services. What 2024-2026 pattern is best?
A) One static staging environment for all PRs
B) Dynamic environment per PR using: GitHub Actions deploying to ephemeral namespaces via vcluster or Uber's devpod, Terraform/Terragrunt

C) No preview environments
D) Developers test only on localhost
Q29. How do you optimize a CI/CD pipeline that takes 45 minutes for a Python app to
under 10 minutes?
A) Buy faster laptop
B) Parallelize tests (pytest-xdist, matrix strategy), implement Docker layer caching + dependency caching (pip cache, actions/cache), use slimmer
base images, remove redundant steps, use merge queues, fail-fast, and remote caching; profile with pipeline observability (e.g., GitHub Actions
metrics, BuildJet)
C) Remove all tests
D) Add sleep 1h in pipeline
Q30. For pipeline observability and DORA metrics compliance, what should you track and
with what tools in 2025?
A) Only count number of commits
B) Track DORA: Deployment Frequency, Lead Time for Changes, Change Failure Rate, MTTR; use tools like GitHub Insights/DORA metrics API,
LinearB/ Sleuth/ Haystack, OpenTelemetry traces for pipeline runs, Datadog CI Visibility, Grafana dashboards on OTLP pipeline metrics
C) No metrics needed
D) Only track coffee consumed
Answer Key - Advanced
| Q | Answer | Explanation |
|---|--------|-------------|
| Q21 | A | Modern 2025 pattern: build-push-action v6 with multi-arch, gha cache, provenance attestation via SLSA & Sigstore, Trivy scan. |
| Q22 | A | GitOps principle: CI is push-based (updates Git), CD is pull-based (ArgoCD syncs). Never kubectl apply from CI to prod directly. |
| Q23 | B | Affected graph + remote caching is standard for Bazel/Nx/Turborepo monorepos to achieve scalability. |
| Q24 | B | SLSA 3 requires non-falsifiable provenance + SBOM + signing + policy enforcement. |
| Q25 | B | Supply chain security requires multiple gates: SCA, provenance, malicious package detection, lockfile enforcement. |
| Q26 | B | GitHub Actions Importer + ARC runner scale + reusable workflows is official enterprise migration path. |
